If You Run Long or Short

Running long: what to protect and what to cut first
  • Protect: the before-and-after EXPLAIN ANALYZE; the composite index; plan_reading through TODO 2; WITH CHECK OPTION; report_views through TODO 1.
  • Cut first (saves 8m): the clinic's EXPLAIN part; keep the debug handouts, or send them home with the README's expected errors.
  • Cut second (saves 4m): 6.1 demo steps 10 to 12 (self-check Q5 and Q6 cover them).
  • Cut third (saves 3m): the 6.2 security step: say "a view can be the only thing an account may read; Lesson 7.4 shows it".
Running short: what to add
  • The lesson 6.1 project's Step 4 live: report C before and after the three-column index.
  • The lesson 6.2 bonus: fake a materialized view with CREATE TABLE ... AS SELECT, insert an order, compare table and view.
  • The 6.2 worked example: a view that filters a window function (Session 8 meets Session 9).
Instructor Kit · Facilitator Guide

Session 10: Normalization and Stored Programs

How to teach design and stored programs in one session: make a flat sheet corrupt itself, find its dependencies and key, split it into 3NF tables and prove with EXCEPT that nothing was lost; then save logic next to the data as procedures and functions, with parameters, characteristics and input checks that refuse bad calls.

2 lessons 3 hrs session Lessons 6.3–7.1 Session 10 of 14 · Part 2

How to Use This Guide

  • Same rhythm as every session: frame → live demo → guided lab → start the lesson's own project. Exercises, quizzes, journals and the rest of each project go home.
  • Every SQL block below was run on MySQL 9.7 to produce the output printed under it: Lesson 6.3 in its own practice database normalize_lab (the lesson's choice; bookstore is not touched), Lesson 7.1 on a freshly loaded bookstore.
  • Keep lesson_16.html (6.3) and lesson_17.html (7.1) open: the dependency diagrams, the ER diagrams and the characteristics table are worth showing, not redrawing. Labs live in labs/module-10/.
  • Two modes in one session. Lesson 6.3 is design on paper and in a throwaway database; 7.1 is programming inside the bookstore. The break is the switch: everyone runs SOURCE bookstore.sql before the 7.1 demo.
  • Teal blocks are hands-on, amber blocks are watch-outs. Lesson codes (6.3, 7.1) match the course site.

Before the Session

  • ☐ Files to hand out: labs/module-10/ without the *_solution.sql files; the workbook.
  • ☐ Run the kit check once: python3 labs/check_labs.py module-10 (10 files, a few seconds). debug_routines.sql needs binary logging (the MySQL default): on a server started with --skip-log-bin its section B does not fail and the check reports it.
  • ☐ Projector machine: MySQL 9.7, no normalize_lab yet (drop it if a rehearsal left one), the client at 18 pt, both lesson pages open at the setup blocks you will paste.
  • ☐ Board, before learners arrive: "the key, the whole key, and nothing but the key" with 1NF / 2NF / 3NF under it; for 7.1, two boxes "procedure: CALL, result sets, OUT, changes data" and "function: one value, inside a query".
  • ☐ Print or share: the workbook; plain paper for the clinic's design sketches; sticky notes for the exit ticket.
Known traps today
SymptomCauseFix
The prompt shows -> after a CREATE PROCEDUREDELIMITER // is still active, or the body's END has no //Type // and Enter, then DELIMITER ;.
Two ERROR 1064 lines for one procedureNo delimiter change: the client cut the body at the first ;Wrap it in DELIMITER // ... END // ... DELIMITER ;.
ERROR 1418 for a functionNo characteristic, and binary logging is onDETERMINISTIC, NO SQL or READS SQL DATA: the true one.
ERROR 1304 ... already exists on the second runNo DROP PROCEDURE IF EXISTS before the CREATEEvery routine in a script starts with its DROP ... IF EXISTS.
A routine returns every row instead of oneA parameter has a column's name (WHERE id = id)p_ and v_ prefixes, or qualify columns with the alias.
ERROR 3780 on a foreign keyReferencing and referenced columns have different types (BIGINT vs INT)One id type everywhere; INT NOT NULL here.
phpMyAdmin users have no DELIMITER lineThe SQL tab has a Delimiter box under the query areaSet it to // there (the lesson's tip).

Opening Segment

0:00

Warm-up: Indexes and Views

10 min
Sequence
  • Retrieval (6m). W1 to W5 on the workbook's Warm-up box, no notes, three minutes; then the answers, W1 and W2 run on the projector.
  • Spreadsheets (4m). Session 9's homework: "find a value that repeats on many rows". Two volunteers describe theirs in one sentence (a sign-up sheet with the trainer's phone on every row; an expense list with the project manager's name). Write both on the board: they come back in the clinic as design exercises.
Warm-up answers (W1 to W5)
  1. W1 Which plan line do you expect, and why is there an index at all? Answer: Index lookup on orders using fk_orders_customer: InnoDB created that index for the foreign key.
  2. W2 Is this view updatable? Answer: YES: each view row is one books row (no grouping, DISTINCT or UNION).
  3. W3 An index on (customer_id, sold_on): can a query on a sold_on range alone seek with it? Answer: No: the dates are sorted only within each customer (leftmost prefix).
  4. W4 What does MySQL store when you create a view? Answer: Only the query; rows are computed on every read.
  5. W5 What does WITH CHECK OPTION refuse? Answer: A write through the view that would produce a row the view cannot show (1369).

Lesson Plans

6.3

Database Design & Normalization

55 min
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Recognize update, insert and delete anomalies, and show each one with a query
  • Find the functional dependencies and the key of a table
  • Normalize a flat table to first, second and third normal form (1NF, 2NF, 3NF)
  • Build the normalized tables with INSERT ... SELECT DISTINCT and prove with EXCEPT that no data was lost
  • Read an ER diagram, and decide when storing derived or duplicated data is worth the risk
Timing in the room
10mFrame: one big sheet corrupts itself; dependencies; 1NF, 2NF, 3NF
20mLive demo: anomalies, 1NF, a dependency test, 3NF, four tables, EXCEPT, a drifting total
15mGuided lab: anomalies_starter.sql
10mStart the lesson project: book_club.sql Steps 1 to 3
Mental model to say

Start from the warm-up spreadsheets: "Every one of these stores the same fact on many rows. It works until someone changes one copy. A database cannot stop people typing wrong values, but a good design makes sure each fact is typed once, so there is nothing to keep in sync."

Then dependencies, as arrows on the board: "A → B means: same A, same B. order_id → order_date, email → name. They come from the rules of the business, not from today's rows." Point at the board line: 1NF one value per cell and a key; 2NF nothing depends on part of a composite key; 3NF nothing depends on a non-key column. "The key, the whole key, and nothing but the key."

Last, the practical recipe: one table per kind of thing, a surrogate id plus UNIQUE on the natural identifier, foreign keys on the many side, a junction table for many-to-many. "This is exactly the bookstore you have used since Module 4."

Live demo (you type, they follow)

The lesson's own sequence in normalize_lab. The long setup blocks are pasted from the lesson page; the short ones are typed.

  1. The first-year order sheet (3m). "Five real orders, the way a spreadsheet holds them: the customer typed on every row, the books squeezed into one cell." Paste the setup from the lesson page (it creates normalize_lab; bookstore is not touched). 
    CREATE DATABASE normalize_lab CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci;
USE normalize_lab;
CREATE TABLE order_sheet (
    order_id     INT PRIMARY KEY,
    order_date   DATE NOT NULL,
    cust_name    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    cust_email   VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    cust_city    VARCHAR(50),
    book_titles  VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
    book_prices  VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    quantities   VARCHAR(50)  NOT NULL,
    order_total  DECIMAL(8, 2) NOT NULL
);
INSERT INTO order_sheet VALUES
    (1, '2026-01-15', 'Alice Johnson', 'alice@example.com', 'New York', 'Dune, 1984', '14.99, 9.99', '1, 2', 34.97),
    (2, '2026-02-20', 'Alice Johnson', 'alice@example.com', 'New York', 'Pride and Prejudice', '7.99', '1', 7.99),
    (3, '2026-03-10', 'Bob Smith', 'bob@example.com', 'Chicago', 'Dune', '14.99', '1', 14.99),
    (4, '2026-01-22', 'Dave Brown', 'dave@example.com', 'Houston', 'The Hobbit, Lord of the Rings', '10.99, 15.99', '1, 1', 26.98),
    (7, '2026-03-28', 'Bob Smith', 'bob@example.com', 'Chicago', '1984, Animal Farm', '9.99, 8.49', '1, 1', 18.48);
SELECT order_id, cust_name, cust_email, book_titles, quantities, order_total
FROM order_sheet
ORDER BY order_id;
    Database changed
+----------+---------------+-------------------+-------------------------------+------------+-------------+
| order_id | cust_name     | cust_email        | book_titles                   | quantities | order_total |
+----------+---------------+-------------------+-------------------------------+------------+-------------+
|        1 | Alice Johnson | alice@example.com | Dune, 1984                    | 1, 2       |       34.97 |
|        2 | Alice Johnson | alice@example.com | Pride and Prejudice           | 1          |        7.99 |
|        3 | Bob Smith     | bob@example.com   | Dune                          | 1          |       14.99 |
|        4 | Dave Brown    | dave@example.com  | The Hobbit, Lord of the Rings | 1, 1       |       26.98 |
|        7 | Bob Smith     | bob@example.com   | 1984, Animal Farm             | 1, 1       |       18.48 |
+----------+---------------+-------------------+-------------------------------+------------+-------------+
  2. A simple question it cannot answer (1m). "How many copies of 1984? The answer is in there, but no aggregate can add up 'the second number in a list'." 
    SELECT order_id, book_titles, quantities
FROM order_sheet
WHERE book_titles LIKE '%1984%'
ORDER BY order_id;
    +----------+-------------------+------------+
| order_id | book_titles       | quantities |
+----------+-------------------+------------+
|        1 | Dune, 1984        | 1, 2       |
|        7 | 1984, Animal Farm | 1, 1       |
+----------+-------------------+------------+
  3. Update anomaly (2m). "Alice changes her email, and one row gets updated." Inside a transaction, rolled back. 
    START TRANSACTION;
UPDATE order_sheet SET cust_email = 'alice.j@example.com' WHERE order_id = 2;
SELECT DISTINCT cust_name, cust_email
FROM order_sheet
WHERE cust_name = 'Alice Johnson';
ROLLBACK;
    +---------------+---------------------+
| cust_name     | cust_email          |
+---------------+---------------------+
| Alice Johnson | alice@example.com   |
| Alice Johnson | alice.j@example.com |
+---------------+---------------------+
  4. Insert anomaly (1m). "Carol signs up for the newsletter. Where does she go?" 
    INSERT INTO order_sheet (cust_name, cust_email, cust_city)
VALUES ('Carol Williams', 'carol@example.com', 'Los Angeles');
    ERROR 1364 (HY000): Field 'order_id' doesn't have a default value
    Every row is an order: no order, no row. Inventing a fake order or making columns nullable makes the data lie.
  5. Delete anomaly (1m). "Cancel Dave's only order, and the store forgets Dave." 
    START TRANSACTION;
DELETE FROM order_sheet WHERE order_id = 4;
SELECT COUNT(*) AS rows_about_dave
FROM order_sheet
WHERE cust_name = 'Dave Brown';
ROLLBACK;
    +-----------------+
| rows_about_dave |
+-----------------+
|               0 |
+-----------------+
  6. 1NF: one value per cell (3m). "One row per book per order. The question that defeated the sheet is an ordinary GROUP BY now." Point at the key (order_id, book_title), and at Alice, now on three rows: 1NF fixes querying, not duplication. 
    CREATE TABLE order_lines_1nf (
    order_id    INT NOT NULL,
    order_date  DATE NOT NULL,
    cust_name   VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    cust_email  VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    cust_city   VARCHAR(50),
    book_title  VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL,
    book_price  DECIMAL(8, 2) NOT NULL,
    quantity    INT NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (order_id, book_title)
);
INSERT INTO order_lines_1nf VALUES
    (1, '2026-01-15', 'Alice Johnson', 'alice@example.com', 'New York', 'Dune', 14.99, 1),
    (1, '2026-01-15', 'Alice Johnson', 'alice@example.com', 'New York', '1984', 9.99, 2),
    (2, '2026-02-20', 'Alice Johnson', 'alice@example.com', 'New York', 'Pride and Prejudice', 7.99, 1),
    (3, '2026-03-10', 'Bob Smith', 'bob@example.com', 'Chicago', 'Dune', 14.99, 1),
    (4, '2026-01-22', 'Dave Brown', 'dave@example.com', 'Houston', 'The Hobbit', 10.99, 1),
    (4, '2026-01-22', 'Dave Brown', 'dave@example.com', 'Houston', 'Lord of the Rings', 15.99, 1),
    (7, '2026-03-28', 'Bob Smith', 'bob@example.com', 'Chicago', '1984', 9.99, 1),
    (7, '2026-03-28', 'Bob Smith', 'bob@example.com', 'Chicago', 'Animal Farm', 8.49, 1);
SELECT book_title, SUM(quantity) AS copies
FROM order_lines_1nf
GROUP BY book_title
ORDER BY copies DESC, book_title;
    +---------------------+--------+
| book_title          | copies |
+---------------------+--------+
| 1984                |      3 |
| Dune                |      2 |
| Animal Farm         |      1 |
| Lord of the Rings   |      1 |
| Pride and Prejudice |      1 |
| The Hobbit          |      1 |
+---------------------+--------+
  7. Test a dependency (1m). "book_title → book_price: no title may have two prices. Empty set means no counterexample. Data can disprove a rule, never prove one." 
    SELECT book_title, COUNT(DISTINCT book_price) AS prices
FROM order_lines_1nf
GROUP BY book_title
HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT book_price) > 1;
    Empty set
  8. 3NF: what the customer columns really depend on (2m). "In the orders the key is order_id, but the name and city are facts about the customer, reached through the email. Three customers, each once." (2NF's two DISTINCT queries are on the lesson page: say them, do not type them.) 
    SELECT DISTINCT cust_email, cust_name, cust_city
FROM order_lines_1nf
ORDER BY cust_email;
    +-------------------+---------------+-----------+
| cust_email        | cust_name     | cust_city |
+-------------------+---------------+-----------+
| alice@example.com | Alice Johnson | New York  |
| bob@example.com   | Bob Smith     | Chicago   |
| dave@example.com  | Dave Brown    | Houston   |
+-------------------+---------------+-----------+
  9. Build the four tables (3m). Paste the block from the lesson page. "Parents first: customers and books, with UNIQUE on the natural identifiers. Then orders and order_items, joining back on email and title to find the new ids." 
    CREATE TABLE customers (
    id     INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    name   VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    email  VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    city   VARCHAR(50)
);
INSERT INTO customers (name, email, city)
SELECT DISTINCT cust_name, cust_email, cust_city
FROM order_lines_1nf
ORDER BY cust_email;
CREATE TABLE books (
    id     INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    title  VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    price  DECIMAL(8, 2) NOT NULL
);
INSERT INTO books (title, price)
SELECT DISTINCT book_title, book_price
FROM order_lines_1nf
ORDER BY book_title;
CREATE TABLE orders (
    id           INT PRIMARY KEY,
    customer_id  INT NOT NULL,
    order_date   DATE NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT fk_lab_orders_customer
        FOREIGN KEY (customer_id) REFERENCES customers(id)
);
INSERT INTO orders (id, customer_id, order_date)
SELECT DISTINCT l.order_id, c.id, l.order_date
FROM order_lines_1nf AS l
JOIN customers AS c ON c.email = l.cust_email;
CREATE TABLE order_items (
    order_id       INT NOT NULL,
    book_id        INT NOT NULL,
    quantity       INT NOT NULL,
    price_at_time  DECIMAL(8, 2) NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (order_id, book_id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_lab_items_order FOREIGN KEY (order_id) REFERENCES orders(id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_lab_items_book  FOREIGN KEY (book_id)  REFERENCES books(id)
);
INSERT INTO order_items (order_id, book_id, quantity, price_at_time)
SELECT l.order_id, b.id, l.quantity, l.book_price
FROM order_lines_1nf AS l
JOIN books AS b ON b.title = l.book_title;
SELECT (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM customers)   AS customers,
       (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM books)       AS books,
       (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM orders)      AS orders,
       (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM order_items) AS order_items;
    +-----------+-------+--------+-------------+
| customers | books | orders | order_items |
+-----------+-------+--------+-------------+
|         3 |     6 |      5 |           8 |
+-----------+-------+--------+-------------+
  10. Prove nothing was lost (2m). "EXCEPT returns the rows of the first query missing from the second. Both directions empty: same rows on both sides." 
    SELECT order_id, order_date, cust_name, cust_email, cust_city, book_title, book_price, quantity
FROM order_lines_1nf
EXCEPT
SELECT o.id, o.order_date, c.name, c.email, c.city, b.title, oi.price_at_time, oi.quantity
FROM orders AS o
JOIN customers   AS c  ON c.id = o.customer_id
JOIN order_items AS oi ON oi.order_id = o.id
JOIN books       AS b  ON b.id = oi.book_id;
SELECT o.id, o.order_date, c.name, c.email, c.city, b.title, oi.price_at_time, oi.quantity
FROM orders AS o
JOIN customers   AS c  ON c.id = o.customer_id
JOIN order_items AS oi ON oi.order_id = o.id
JOIN books       AS b  ON b.id = oi.book_id
EXCEPT
SELECT order_id, order_date, cust_name, cust_email, cust_city, book_title, book_price, quantity
FROM order_lines_1nf;
    Empty set
Empty set
  11. Silent: a stored total drifts (1m). "Add the total back 'for speed', then fix a quantity the ordinary way. No error anywhere, and order 1 has two totals." This is why the bookstore has no stored total. 
    ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN total DECIMAL(8, 2);
UPDATE orders AS o
SET total = (SELECT SUM(quantity * price_at_time) FROM order_items WHERE order_id = o.id);
UPDATE order_items SET quantity = 3
WHERE order_id = 1 AND book_id = (SELECT id FROM books WHERE title = '1984');
SELECT o.id, o.total AS stored_total, SUM(oi.quantity * oi.price_at_time) AS real_total
FROM orders AS o
JOIN order_items AS oi ON oi.order_id = o.id
GROUP BY o.id, o.total
ORDER BY o.id;
    +----+--------------+------------+
| id | stored_total | real_total |
+----+--------------+------------+
|  1 |        34.97 |      44.96 |
|  2 |         7.99 |       7.99 |
|  3 |        14.99 |      14.99 |
|  4 |        26.98 |      26.98 |
|  7 |        18.48 |      18.48 |
+----+--------------+------------+
    Undo or not: the lab database is throwaway. DROP DATABASE normalize_lab; when the lesson is over.
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • Confusing 2NF and 3NF. Coach with one question per column: "Does it need the whole key, part of the key, or another non-key column?" Part = 2NF, another column = 3NF.
  • Treating data as proof. "The query found no counterexample, so it is a rule." Coach: "Would the business allow two prices for one title next year?" Data can only disprove.
  • Calling price_at_time redundant. Coach: "What does each column answer?" Today's price versus what this customer paid on this order: two facts (self-check and lesson quiz).
Guided lab

File: labs/module-10/l6_3-normalization/anomalies_starter.sql; reference labs/module-10/l6_3-normalization/anomalies_solution.sql. A new sheet: the bookstore's workshop sign-ups (attendee, workshop, date, room, host, fee). It builds kit_s10_sheet and drops it on every run.

  • TODO 1 together (4m): the update anomaly. Ask first: "if only Bob's row changes, how many dates does the workshop have?"
  • TODO 2 in pairs (4m): the delete anomaly: Frank cancels, and the store forgets Speed Reading exists.
  • TODO 3 in pairs (5m): two dependency tests in the style of the first. Circulate for HAVING written as WHERE (1111, invalid use of group function).
  • Expected: two dates for Bookbinding Basics; 0 rows about Speed Reading; one value of each per workshop; host_email → host_name no counterexample; host_email → workshop broken by Lena, who hosts 2 workshops; key check 6 rows, 6 pairs, 5 attendees.
  • Debrief (2m): "Write the dependencies as arrows. What is the key? Which tables does that suggest?" (attendees, hosts, workshops, signups).
  • Homework / fast finishers: labs/module-10/l6_3-normalization/normalize_starter.sql: the same sheet migrated to those four tables and proved lossless with EXCEPT.
Start the lesson project

The lesson project is Normalize the Book Club Sheet, book_club.sql. In the room: Steps 1 to 3 (10 minutes).

  • Step 1: import the sheet into normalize_lab (paste from the lesson page).
  • Step 2: write the dependencies and the key as comments BEFORE reading the lesson's answer; then run the check query.
  • Step 3: create members, leaders, clubs and memberships.
  • Goes home: Step 4 (migrate, parents first), Step 5 (EXCEPT both ways), Step 6 (move the Sci-Fi Circle to Wednesday: one row).
Checks for understanding

Ask these out loud:

  • Which normal form does a comma list in a cell break? Answer: 1NF.
  • What is the key of the order lines table? Answer: (order_id, book_title): neither column alone determines the rest.
  • Why does the bookstore store no order total? Answer: it can always be computed, and a stored copy can drift (demo step 11).
  • When is denormalizing reasonable? Answer: for a measured slow query, after indexes and a better query failed, with a plan to keep the copy in sync.

Workbook self-check, items Q1 to Q6.

Answers: Q1 A) An update anomaly; Q2 C) First normal form: a repeating group of columns; Q3 B) 2NF: course_room depends on only part of the key; Q4 dept_id → dept_name (a transitive dependency: id → dept_id → dept_name); move dept_name to departments(id, name) and keep dept_id as a foreign key; Q5 D) The four tables, joined back, hold exactly the original rows: the split lost nothing and added nothing; Q6 B) The data does not contradict member_email → member_name; whether it is a rule is a business decision. Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: a printed dependency template ("___ → ___ because ___") for TODO 3 and the project's Step 2. Pair learners who found the warm-up spreadsheets easy with those who did not.

Stretch: the lesson's Exercise 4 (design a recipe site end to end) and the bonus (a foreign key to bookstore.books from another database: what happens on a reload?).

7.1

Stored Procedures & Functions

70 min
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Explain what the DELIMITER client command does, and when you do not need it
  • Create and call stored procedures with IN, OUT and INOUT parameters
  • Write stored functions with the right characteristic (DETERMINISTIC, READS SQL DATA) and use them inside queries
  • Use local variables, IF, CASE and WHILE in a routine, and reject bad input with SIGNAL
  • List, inspect, replace and drop routines, and read the errors MySQL raises for broken ones (1064, 1418, 1172, 1304, 1644)
Timing in the room
10mFrame: logic next to the data; procedure or function; DELIMITER
25mLive demo: DELIMITER, IN, OUT, local variables, IF, a function, 1418, SIGNAL, WHERE id = id, ROUTINES
20mGuided lab: procedures_starter.sql
15mStart the lesson project: bookstore_routines.sql Steps 1 and 2
Mental model to say

"Until now every query lived in your head or in a file. When five programs need the same ten-line query, someone copies it five times and the copies drift: the same problem as this morning's spreadsheet, in code. A stored routine is a named block of SQL saved next to the tables, with parameters. Everyone calls the one copy."

Point at the two boxes: a procedure is run with CALL, may return result sets, hands values back through OUT parameters and may change data; a function returns one value and lives inside a query, like ROUND or CONCAT.

Then the one confusing thing, said plainly: "The mysql client cuts your typing at every semicolon. A body has several. DELIMITER // tells the CLIENT to wait for // for a while; the server never sees the word."

Live demo (you type, they follow)

Everyone types SOURCE bookstore.sql first. The lesson's own examples follow; type steps 1 and 8 (DELIMITER and SIGNAL), paste the rest.

  1. DELIMITER, and a first procedure (3m). "The client sends your text to the server at every semicolon. A body has several, so for a while we tell the CLIENT to wait for // instead. The server never sees the word DELIMITER." Point at the two result sets: each SELECT in the body sends one back. 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE PROCEDURE say_hello()
BEGIN
    SELECT 'Hello from the bookstore!' AS greeting;
    SELECT COUNT(*) AS books_in_stock FROM books WHERE in_stock = TRUE;
END //
DELIMITER ;
CALL say_hello();
    +---------------------------+
| greeting                  |
+---------------------------+
| Hello from the bookstore! |
+---------------------------+
+----------------+
| books_in_stock |
+----------------+
|              9 |
+----------------+
  2. IN: a parameter (2m). "p_ marks a parameter. You will see in a minute why the prefix matters." 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE PROCEDURE books_by_author(IN p_author VARCHAR(100))
BEGIN
    SELECT b.title, b.price
    FROM books AS b
    JOIN authors AS a ON a.id = b.author_id
    WHERE a.name = p_author
    ORDER BY b.title;
END //
DELIMITER ;
CALL books_by_author('George Orwell');
    +-------------+-------+
| title       | price |
+-------------+-------+
| 1984        |  9.99 |
| Animal Farm |  8.49 |
+-------------+-------+
  3. OUT: an answer through a user variable (2m). "SELECT ... INTO stores instead of printing, so the CALL prints nothing. The answer lands in @tolkien_books." 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE PROCEDURE count_books_by(IN p_author VARCHAR(100), OUT p_count INT)
BEGIN
    SELECT COUNT(*) INTO p_count
    FROM books AS b
    JOIN authors AS a ON a.id = b.author_id
    WHERE a.name = p_author;
END //
DELIMITER ;
CALL count_books_by('J.R.R. Tolkien', @tolkien_books);
SELECT @tolkien_books AS tolkien_books;
    +---------------+
| tolkien_books |
+---------------+
|             2 |
+---------------+
  4. Local variables (3m). "DECLARE comes first in a block; a local variable lives for one call." 34.97 plus 8% tax. 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE PROCEDURE order_receipt(IN p_order_id INT)
BEGIN
    DECLARE v_subtotal DECIMAL(10, 2) DEFAULT 0;
    DECLARE v_tax_rate DECIMAL(4, 3) DEFAULT 0.080;
    DECLARE v_tax      DECIMAL(10, 2);
    SELECT COALESCE(SUM(quantity * price_at_time), 0) INTO v_subtotal
    FROM order_items
    WHERE order_id = p_order_id;
    SET v_tax = ROUND(v_subtotal * v_tax_rate, 2);
    SELECT p_order_id         AS order_id,
           v_subtotal         AS subtotal,
           v_tax              AS tax,
           v_subtotal + v_tax AS total;
END //
DELIMITER ;
CALL order_receipt(1);
    +----------+----------+------+-------+
| order_id | subtotal | tax  | total |
+----------+----------+------+-------+
|        1 |    34.97 | 2.80 | 37.77 |
+----------+----------+------+-------+
  5. IF ... ELSEIF ... ELSE (3m). "Gold from $50, Silver from $30, Bronze above zero, New otherwise." 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE PROCEDURE classify_customer(IN p_customer_id INT, OUT p_tier VARCHAR(10))
BEGIN
    DECLARE v_spent DECIMAL(10, 2);
    SELECT COALESCE(SUM(oi.quantity * oi.price_at_time), 0) INTO v_spent
    FROM orders AS o
    JOIN order_items AS oi ON oi.order_id = o.id
    WHERE o.customer_id = p_customer_id;
    IF v_spent >= 50 THEN
        SET p_tier = 'Gold';
    ELSEIF v_spent >= 30 THEN
        SET p_tier = 'Silver';
    ELSEIF v_spent > 0 THEN
        SET p_tier = 'Bronze';
    ELSE
        SET p_tier = 'New';
    END IF;
END //
DELIMITER ;
CALL classify_customer(1, @alice);
CALL classify_customer(2, @bob);
CALL classify_customer(3, @carol);
SELECT @alice AS alice, @bob AS bob, @carol AS carol;
    +-------+--------+-------+
| alice | bob    | carol |
+-------+--------+-------+
| Gold  | Silver | New   |
+-------+--------+-------+
  6. A stored function (3m). "One value, used inside a query like a built-in function. The characteristics line describes it to the server: same input, same output (DETERMINISTIC), no table read (NO SQL)." 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE FUNCTION with_tax(p_amount DECIMAL(10, 2))
RETURNS DECIMAL(10, 2)
DETERMINISTIC
NO SQL
BEGIN
    RETURN ROUND(p_amount * 1.08, 2);
END //
DELIMITER ;
SELECT title, price, with_tax(price) AS price_with_tax
FROM books
WHERE price >= 11
ORDER BY price DESC;
    +-----------------------+-------+----------------+
| title                 | price | price_with_tax |
+-----------------------+-------+----------------+
| Lord of the Rings     | 15.99 |          17.27 |
| Dune                  | 14.99 |          16.19 |
| To Kill a Mockingbird | 11.99 |          12.95 |
+-----------------------+-------+----------------+
  7. Without characteristics (1m). "Binary logging is on by default since 8.0, so the server wants to know what a function does." 
    CREATE FUNCTION book_count()
RETURNS INT
RETURN (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM books);
    ERROR 1418 (HY000): This function has none of DETERMINISTIC, NO SQL, or READS SQL DATA in its declaration and binary logging is enabled (you *might* want to use the less safe log_bin_trust_function_creators variable)
    Fix: READS SQL DATA after RETURNS INT. Never turn on log_bin_trust_function_creators to silence it.
  8. Check first, then change: SIGNAL (4m). "A procedure that changes data checks its input before the UPDATE. SIGNAL raises your own error, number 1644, with your message." 
    DELIMITER //
CREATE PROCEDURE set_book_price(IN p_book_id INT, IN p_price DECIMAL(8, 2))
BEGIN
    IF p_price IS NULL OR p_price <= 0 THEN
        SIGNAL SQLSTATE '45000'
            SET MESSAGE_TEXT = 'Price must be greater than zero';
    END IF;
    IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM books WHERE id = p_book_id) THEN
        SIGNAL SQLSTATE '45000'
            SET MESSAGE_TEXT = 'No book with that id';
    END IF;
    UPDATE books SET price = p_price WHERE id = p_book_id;
END //
DELIMITER ;
CALL set_book_price(5, -1.00);
CALL set_book_price(99, 9.99);
    ERROR 1644 (45000): Price must be greater than zero
ERROR 1644 (45000): No book with that id
    Both checks run before the UPDATE, so a refused call changes nothing.
  9. Silent: a parameter named like a column (2m). "Book 1, please." Run it before explaining. "Inside the routine, a bare id is the parameter, so WHERE id = id is 1 = 1 on every row. Picture this in a DELETE." 
    CREATE PROCEDURE book_by_id(IN id INT)
    SELECT b.id, b.title FROM books AS b WHERE id = id ORDER BY b.id;
CALL book_by_id(1);
    +----+------------------------+
| id | title                  |
+----+------------------------+
|  1 | Dune                   |
|  2 | 1984                   |
|  3 | To Kill a Mockingbird  |
|  4 | The Great Gatsby       |
|  5 | Pride and Prejudice    |
|  6 | The Hobbit             |
|  7 | Brave New World        |
|  8 | The Catcher in the Rye |
|  9 | Lord of the Rings      |
| 10 | Fahrenheit 451         |
| 11 | Animal Farm            |
+----+------------------------+
  10. What the database now holds (2m). "Procedures say CONTAINS SQL unless declared otherwise. Reloading bookstore.sql wipes all of them: routines live in a script file." 
    SELECT ROUTINE_NAME, ROUTINE_TYPE, IS_DETERMINISTIC, SQL_DATA_ACCESS
FROM information_schema.ROUTINES
WHERE ROUTINE_SCHEMA = 'bookstore'
ORDER BY ROUTINE_TYPE, ROUTINE_NAME;
    +-------------------+--------------+------------------+-----------------+
| ROUTINE_NAME      | ROUTINE_TYPE | IS_DETERMINISTIC | SQL_DATA_ACCESS |
+-------------------+--------------+------------------+-----------------+
| with_tax          | FUNCTION     | YES              | NO SQL          |
| books_by_author   | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
| book_by_id        | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
| classify_customer | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
| count_books_by    | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
| order_receipt     | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
| say_hello         | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
| set_book_price    | PROCEDURE    | NO               | CONTAINS SQL    |
+-------------------+--------------+------------------+-----------------+
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • A stuck -> prompt. Coach: "Which delimiter is active? Type // to send, then DELIMITER ;." Every kit example switches back in the same block.
  • Expecting OUT parameters to print. "CALL printed nothing." Coach: "Where did you ask it to put the answer? SELECT @your_variable."
  • SIGNAL after the change. A check placed after the UPDATE stops the procedure too late. Coach: "Which statements already ran when SIGNAL fires?" (Lesson 7.2's transactions handle multi-step changes.)
Guided lab

File: labs/module-10/l7_1-stored-procedures/procedures_starter.sql; reference labs/module-10/l7_1-stored-procedures/procedures_solution.sql. Three new procedures for the store's admin page; every routine has its DROP ... IF EXISTS, so the file reruns after every edit.

  • TODO 1 together (5m): category_shelf returns the three cheapest books of any category. Add the WHERE on the parameter, delete the LIMIT, SOURCE again.
  • TODO 2 in pairs (7m): author_stats's second OUT parameter. Ask them to try Isaac Asimov (author 10) with and without COALESCE.
  • TODO 3 in pairs (6m): the percent guard, placed BEFORE the other one; then CALL raise_price(6, 80); at the prompt.
  • Expected: Dystopian: Brave New World 9.49, 1984 9.99, Fahrenheit 451 10.49; Orwell 2 books 6 copies, Asimov 0 and 0; the guard prints ERROR 1644 (45000): Percent must be between 1 and 50 and The Hobbit stays 10.99; the good call shows 12.09 inside the transaction, 10.99 after ROLLBACK.
  • Debrief (2m): "Which of the three changes data? What makes it safe to call with bad input?"
  • Homework / fast finishers: labs/module-10/l7_1-stored-procedures/functions_starter.sql: shipping_fee (DETERMINISTIC, NO SQL) and copies_sold (READS SQL DATA).
Start the lesson project

The lesson project is The Bookstore Routine Library, bookstore_routines.sql: two functions and two procedures in one rerunnable script. In the room: Steps 1 and 2 (15 minutes).

  • Step 1: order_total(p_order_id), READS SQL DATA, used for Alice's three orders (34.97, 7.99, 15.99).
  • Step 2: customer_tier(p_customer_id), the demo's IF as a function with a CASE in RETURN.
  • Done in the room = both functions in the file, each after its DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS, and the file runs twice.
  • Goes home: Step 3 (customer_report: two result sets), Step 4 (add_book with SIGNAL and LAST_INSERT_ID), Step 5 (break it on purpose), Step 6 (inventory and rerun; test CALLs stay out of the file).
Checks for understanding

Ask these out loud:

  • Who reads DELIMITER //? Answer: the mysql client (and SOURCE); apps, Workbench and phpMyAdmin's Delimiter box do not need you to type it.
  • Why READS SQL DATA and not DETERMINISTIC for order_total? Answer: it reads rows that change; DETERMINISTIC would be a false promise.
  • What does a procedure give back that a function cannot? Answer: result sets, several OUT values, and transactions inside.
  • Why the p_ prefix? Answer: a parameter named like a column silently shadows it (WHERE id = id).

Workbook self-check, items Q7 to Q12.

Answers: Q7 A) Two ERROR 1064 lines: the client sent the text in two pieces at the semicolons; Q8 4 (in a column named cheaper); Q9 D) DETERMINISTIC and NO SQL; Q10 C) ERROR 1172 (42000): Result consisted of more than one row; Q11 ERROR 1644 (45000): Quantity must be at least 1; Q12 C) Run COMMIT or START TRANSACTION. Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: a card with the procedure skeleton (DROP, DELIMITER //, CREATE ... BEGIN, END //, DELIMITER ;) taped to the screen. In the lab, TODO 1 and 2 only.

Stretch: the lesson's Exercise 4 (a WHILE loop up the chain of command, compared with Session 8's recursive CTE) and the bonus (a duplicate guard with a message built in a variable).

Clinic

2:25

Clinic: Design Review and the Debug Handouts

25 min
Timing
10mDesign review: the two warm-up spreadsheets, as dependencies and tables on the board
12mdebug_design.sql and debug_routines.sql in pairs: cause and fix in the workbook table
3mWhole room: one volunteer per error
Design review (10m)
  • Take the first warm-up spreadsheet. The room calls out the dependencies ("trainer_email → trainer_phone"), you write them as arrows, then the key, then the tables. Five minutes, then the second spreadsheet.
  • Ask for one junction table and one natural identifier with UNIQUE in each design. These sketches are the warm-up for the final project's design step (Sessions 12 to 13).
What the handouts print, and the fixes
Database changed
ERROR 1062 (23000): Duplicate entry 'lena.park@bookstore.example' for key 'hosts.email'
ERROR 3780 (HY000): Referencing column 'workshop_id' and referenced column 'id' in foreign key constraint 'fk_dbg_feedback_workshop' are incompatible.
ERROR 1451 (23000): Cannot delete or update a parent row: a foreign key constraint fails (`kit_s10_debug`.`workshops`, CONSTRAINT `fk_dbg_workshops_host` FOREIGN KEY (`host_id`) REFERENCES `hosts` (`id`))
+-------+-----------+
| hosts | workshops |
+-------+-----------+
|     2 |         2 |
+-------+-----------+
  • 1062: the lookup table's UNIQUE refused a second copy of every host: the design working. Fix: migration scripts start from a clean state, or insert only missing rows (WHERE NOT EXISTS).
  • 3780: BIGINT cannot reference INT. Fix: workshop_id INT NOT NULL; one id type everywhere.
  • 1451: Lena still hosts workshops. Fix: decide what happens to her workshops first (reassign them), or mark hosts inactive instead of deleting.
ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax; check the manual that corresponds to your MySQL server version for the right syntax to use near '' at line 3
ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax; check the manual that corresponds to your MySQL server version for the right syntax to use near 'END' at line 1
ERROR 1418 (HY000): This function has none of DETERMINISTIC, NO SQL, or READS SQL DATA in its declaration and binary logging is enabled (you *might* want to use the less safe log_bin_trust_function_creators variable)
ERROR 1172 (42000): Result consisted of more than one row
ERROR 1304 (42000): PROCEDURE dbg_count_books already exists
ERROR 1644 (45000): Pages must be a positive number
+----+----------------+-------+
| id | title          | pages |
+----+----------------+-------+
| 10 | Fahrenheit 451 |   158 |
+----+----------------+-------+
  • 1064 twice (A): no delimiter change. Fix: DELIMITER // ... END // DELIMITER ;, or drop BEGIN/END for a one-statement body.
  • 1418 (B): a function with no characteristic. Fix: READS SQL DATA after RETURNS INT.
  • 1172 (C): Tolkien has two books. Fix: SELECT MIN(title) INTO ... or ORDER BY title LIMIT 1.
  • 1304 (D): created twice. Fix: DROP PROCEDURE IF EXISTS first.
  • 1644 (E): the guard worked; nothing changed (158 pages). Not a bug: the right call is CALL dbg_set_pages(10, 160);.

Closing

Exit ticket (3 questions, no notes, 4 minutes)
  1. order_lines(order_id, book_title, order_date, book_price, quantity), key (order_id, book_title): name one partial dependency and the table it moves to. Answer: order_id → order_date (to orders) or book_title → book_price (to books).
  2. Why does a procedure that changes data check its input before the UPDATE, and how does it refuse? Answer: so a refused call changes nothing; SIGNAL SQLSTATE '45000' SET MESSAGE_TEXT = '...' (error 1644).
  3. Function or procedure: "show each book's price with tax in a SELECT"? "Add a book and its new author, and return the new id"? Answer: a function; a procedure (it changes data and hands back an OUT value).
Homework (about 2 to 3 hours)
  • Finish both lesson projects. book_club.sql Steps 4 to 6; bookstore_routines.sql Steps 3 to 6 (run it twice; test CALLs at the prompt, not in the file).
  • Lesson exercises. 6.3: Exercises 1 to 3 (name the violations, test a dependency, normalize the invoices); 4 is a stretch. 7.1: Exercises 1 to 3 (books in a category, sale prices, city statistics); 4 is a stretch.
  • Both Quick Quizzes and both journal entries.
  • Labs: labs/module-10/l6_3-normalization/normalize_starter.sql, labs/module-10/l7_1-stored-procedures/functions_starter.sql.
  • Before the Next Lesson (from 7.1): make sure you can open two mysql client windows at once (two terminals, or two Workbench tabs): Lesson 7.2 uses both.
Bring list for Session 11
  • A laptop that can run two mysql client sessions side by side (Lesson 7.2's transaction demos), and bookstore.sql.
  • book_club.sql and bookstore_routines.sql, both run twice without an error (the Session 11 homework check; Lesson 7.2 adds a procedure with an error handler to a library like bookstore_routines.sql).
  • Session 11 holds Checkpoint 2 (Modules 5 and 6, 30 minutes): review your CTE, window, index, view and normalization notes. The course folder with labs/, the workbook and the journal.

If You Run Long or Short

Running long: what to protect and what to cut first
  • Protect: the three anomalies; the EXCEPT proof; DELIMITER and SIGNAL; the procedures lab through TODO 2.
  • Cut first (saves 10m): the clinic's design review; keep the debug handouts.
  • Cut second (saves 1m): 6.3 demo step 11 (the drifting total: the lesson's Common Mistake 4).
  • Cut third (saves 3m): 7.1 demo step 5 (IF): the project's customer_tier does the same with CASE.
Running short: what to add
  • The 7.1 project's Step 4 live: add_book creating Ursula K. Le Guin, then Step 5's refused free book.
  • Draw the bookstore's full ER diagram with the room, including the employees' manager relationship (a one-to-many on itself).
  • Session 8 meets Session 10: the 7.1 Exercise 4 WHILE loop next to the 5.1 recursive chain of command.
Instructor Kit · Facilitator Guide

Session 11: Transactions and JSON

How to teach the eleventh session of MySQL Foundations: Checkpoint 2 on Modules 5 and 6 (CTEs, window functions, indexes, views, normalization), then transactions (all or nothing, two silent traps, two windows and a lock timeout, a procedure that rolls back on any error) and JSON columns (validated documents, the two arrows, JSON from rows and back, an indexed path, and when JSON is the wrong tool).

2 lessons 3 hrs session Lessons 7.2–7.3 Session 11 of 14 · Part 2 Checkpoint 2

How to Use This Guide

  • Same rhythm for both lessons: frame (the goal and one mental model) → live demo (you type, they follow) → guided lab (a kit lab, new practice on the lesson's ideas) → start the lesson's own project. The lesson exercises, quiz and journal are homework.
  • Session 11 is Part 2's checkpoint session: Checkpoint 2 (Modules 5 and 6) takes 30 minutes right after the warm-up, so each lesson gets 60 minutes in the room instead of the usual 65 to 75. The lessons are written for 110 and 100 minutes alone; the room keeps the ideas that are hard alone (two windows, the handler, the arrows) and sends the rest home.
  • Every SQL block below was run to produce the output printed under it, on MySQL 9.7 with the default settings, from a fresh bookstore.sql. The two-window table in the 7.2 demo comes from two real client sessions on the same server (the test server's lock timeout is 5 seconds; a default server waits 50).
  • Keep lesson_18.html (7.2) and lesson_json.html (7.3) open in browser tabs for the diagrams. Labs live in labs/module-11/: _starter.sql files are handouts, _solution.sql files and checkpoint-2/checkpoint2_solution.sql are yours.
  • Teal blocks are hands-on, amber blocks are watch-outs. Lesson codes (7.2, 7.3) match the course site.
The #1 risk of Session 11: the checkpoint eats the transactions demo

Thirty minutes of assessment at the start of a session with two dense lessons leaves no slack. Before: print the Checkpoint 2 page (it is in the workbook) or make sure every learner can open it, and copy checkpoint2_starter.sql into everyone's labs/ folder; it must not be handed out earlier. In the room: start at 0:10 sharp, stop at 0:40 sharp, even mid-sentence: unfinished parts are information, not failure. After: mark with the rubric in the Answer Key before Session 12; the warm-up there returns it.

Before the Session

  • ☐ Bring list you can assume (from Session 10): a laptop with MySQL 9.7 and the bookstore database loaded, the root password, the course folder with labs/, Lesson 7.1's bookstore_routines.sql and Lesson 6.3's book_club.sql (homework), the workbook and the journal.
  • ☐ Files: copy labs/module-11/ without the *_solution.sql files and without checkpoint-2/ into the learners' shared folder. Hand out checkpoint-2/checkpoint2_starter.sql at 0:10 (USB stick or the shared folder, opened at 0:10).
  • ☐ Run the kit check once: python3 labs/check_labs.py module-11 from this kit's folder: 12 files, every line PASS.
  • ☐ Projector machine: MySQL 9.7 with a fresh bookstore (SOURCE labs/_states/bookstore.sql), two terminals side by side at 18 pt or larger, both logged in with mysql -u root -p bookstore, labeled A and B. Paste-ready: the lesson's Setup block (accounts) and its transfer procedure; the 7.3 setup (the ALTER TABLE and three UPDATEs). Browser tabs: lesson_18.html, lesson_json.html.
  • ☐ Board, before learners arrive: "START TRANSACTION → … → COMMIT (keep) / ROLLBACK (discard)"; the list "commits by itself: CREATE · ALTER · DROP · TRUNCATE · GRANT"; and two columns headed "column" and "JSON" for the 7.3 frame.
  • ☐ Print or share: the workbook (the Checkpoint 2 page and its Part 5 table), sticky notes for the exit ticket.
Known environment traps
SymptomCauseFix
ERROR 1146 ... Table 'bookstore.accounts' doesn't existThe accounts table is the lesson's own setup, not part of bookstore.sqlRun the lesson's Setup block (the CREATE TABLE accounts and its INSERT) first.
The second window says ERROR 1049 Unknown databaseLogged in without bookstore, or into another server (Docker vs local)mysql -u root -p bookstore in both windows; SELECT VERSION(); in both.
Window B never gives upA default server waits 50 seconds (innodb_lock_wait_timeout)Wait, or COMMIT in A: B then finishes. Do not change the global setting in class.
ERROR 1064 at DELIMITER // in Workbench or phpMyAdminDELIMITER is a mysql-client commandUse the mysql client for procedures (Lesson 7.1), or the tool's own delimiter setting.
ERROR 1060 Duplicate column name 'attributes'The 7.3 setup or catalog_feed.sql ran twice without a reloadSOURCE labs/_states/bookstore.sql, then the setup once.
->> gives ERROR 1064 on a laptopMariaDB (XAMPP) has no arrow operatorsJSON_VALUE(attributes, '$.format') instead; the lesson's MariaDB section.

Opening Segment

0:00

Warm-up: Normalization and Stored Programs

10 min
Timing
6mFive retrieval questions on Lessons 6.3 and 7.1 (workbook Warm-up box), answers by show of hands
4mHomework check: who ran book_club.sql and bookstore_routines.sql twice without errors; one question each
The questions and their answers
  1. W1 (6.3) A book-club sheet has a cell that reads Dune, 1984. Which normal form does that break, and what is the fix? Answer: 1NF: one value per cell. Fix: one row per book in a child table (member_id, book_id).
  2. W2 (6.3) "The key, the whole key, and nothing but the key." Which part of the phrase is 2NF, and which is 3NF? Answer: "The whole key" is 2NF (no column depends on only part of a composite key); "nothing but the key" is 3NF (no column depends on another non-key column).
  3. W3 (7.1) Which program reads the line DELIMITER //: the server, the client or the procedure? Answer: The mysql client. It changes where the client cuts statements; the server never sees the word DELIMITER.
  4. W4 (7.1) You want SELECT title, sale_price(price) FROM books to work. Do you write a stored procedure or a stored function? Answer: A stored function: it returns one value and can sit inside an expression. A procedure runs with CALL.

W5 (7.1) Predict, then run (it creates a small function in bookstore, which the 7.2 demo does not need):

DROP FUNCTION IF EXISTS sale_price;
CREATE FUNCTION sale_price(p_price DECIMAL(8, 2))
RETURNS DECIMAL(8, 2) DETERMINISTIC
RETURN ROUND(p_price * 0.8, 2);
SELECT title, price, sale_price(price) AS on_sale
FROM books
WHERE id IN (1, 2)
ORDER BY id;
+-------+-------+---------+
| title | price | on_sale |
+-------+-------+---------+
| Dune  | 14.99 |   11.99 |
| 1984  |  9.99 |    7.99 |
+-------+-------+---------+

If W3 or W4 split the room, take one minute on the board: the client cuts statements, the server runs them; a function returns a value inside an expression, a procedure is CALLed. Both come back in today's handler.

0:10

Timing

What learners get

The workbook's Checkpoint 2 page and labs/module-11/checkpoint-2/checkpoint2_starter.sql. Your reference is labs/module-11/checkpoint-2/checkpoint2_solution.sql.

PartLessonsTask
Part 15.1, 5.2The best seller(s) of each category by copies sold (SUM of order_items.quantity), ties sharing first place: a CTE for the copies and a window function to rank. Columns category, title, copies, sorted by category.
Part 25.1Everyone who reports to Daniel Reyes (employee 2), directly or through someone else, with a level column (1 = reports to Daniel directly), sorted by level and id: a recursive CTE.

Running it
