Opening Segment

0:00

Warm-up: Transactions and JSON

10 min
Timing
6mFive retrieval questions on Lessons 7.2 and 7.3 (workbook Warm-up box), answers by show of hands
4mCheckpoint 2 returned: each learner reads your sentence of advice; name the one part most of the room should revisit
The questions and their answers
  1. W1 (7.2) START TRANSACTION;, an UPDATE, an ALTER TABLE, then ROLLBACK;. Is the UPDATE undone? Answer: No. ALTER TABLE caused an implicit commit before it ran, so the UPDATE was already committed and the ROLLBACK had nothing to undo.
  2. W2 (7.2) What is the difference between ERROR 1205 and ERROR 1213? Answer: 1205: a lock wait timed out; only the waiting statement failed and the transaction is still open. 1213: a deadlock; InnoDB rolled the whole transaction back, so retry all of it.
  3. W3 (7.3) In WHERE attributes ? '$.format' = 'hardcover', which arrow goes where the ? is, and why? Answer: ->> : it returns plain text; -> would return the JSON string "hardcover" with its quotes, and the comparison would fail.
  4. W4 (7.3) What does JSON_TABLE do, in one sentence? Answer: It turns a JSON array (in a column or a literal) into rows with typed columns that you can join, group and filter.

W5 (7.2 + 7.3) Predict, then run (in a practice database):

CREATE TABLE notes (id INT PRIMARY KEY, doc JSON);
INSERT INTO notes VALUES (1, '{"qty": 2, "tags": ["gift"]}');
START TRANSACTION;
UPDATE notes SET doc = JSON_SET(doc, '$.qty', 5);
ROLLBACK;
SELECT doc->>'$.qty' AS qty, doc->'$.tags' AS tags FROM notes;
+------+----------+
| qty  | tags     |
+------+----------+
| 2    | ["gift"] |
+------+----------+

Checkpoint 2: if more than a third of the room was below Proficient in the same row, assign that lesson's exercises again; Part 3's COUNT(*) slip and Part 1's window function in WHERE come back in the final project's Saturday queries.

Lesson Plans

7.4

User Management & Permissions

65 min
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Create accounts as 'user'@'host' and explain how MySQL 9.7 stores their passwords (caching_sha2_password)
  • Grant and revoke privileges at the global, database, table and column level, and read SHOW GRANTS
  • Log in as a restricted user and recognize the access-denied errors (1142, 1143, 1044, 3118)
  • Group privileges into roles, assign them, and make them active with SET DEFAULT ROLE
  • Design least-privilege accounts for an application, a report and staff, and script them
Timing in the room
10mFrame: why not root; 'user'@'host'; least privilege; GRANT what ON where TO whom
30mLive demo: grants at three levels, logins as each account, REVOKE, roles, a locked account
15mGuided lab: warehouse_accounts_starter.sql in pairs
10mStart the lesson project: team_access.sql Steps 1 to 3
Mental model to say

Start with the three stories from the lesson: a clerk's DELETE without WHERE, an attacker in the search box, a dashboard password in a shared config file. "None of these needs root. Each needs an account that can do its job and nothing more: the principle of least privilege."

Then the building: "root is the master key. A program gets the key to one room; a report gets a window to look through. Every account is a name AND a door: 'user'@'host'."

Then the sentence pattern on the board, GRANT what ON where TO whom, and the levels from wide to narrow: *.* (administrators only), bookstore.*, bookstore.books, a column list. "Grant at the narrowest level that works, and prove it by logging in as the account."

Live demo (you type, they follow)

The lesson's own accounts, from a fresh bookstore.sql. Root window on the left, the second window on the right: log in there as each account when the step says so. Learners type along in their own two windows; the passwords are the lesson's placeholders.

  1. An account (2m). "An account is a user name AND the host it connects from. Always write the host on purpose." 
    CREATE USER 'shop_reports'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED BY 'CHANGE-ME-reports-1';
SELECT user, host, plugin, account_locked
FROM mysql.user
WHERE user = 'shop_reports';
    +--------------+-----------+-----------------------+----------------+
| user         | host      | plugin                | account_locked |
+--------------+-----------+-----------------------+----------------+
| shop_reports | localhost | caching_sha2_password | N              |
+--------------+-----------+-----------------------+----------------+
    The plugin column: caching_sha2_password, the 9.7 default (Session 1's health check).
  2. A new account can do nothing (1m). "USAGE means: may connect. That is all." 
    SHOW GRANTS FOR 'shop_reports'@'localhost';
    +--------------------------------------------------+
| Grants for shop_reports@localhost                |
+--------------------------------------------------+
| GRANT USAGE ON *.* TO `shop_reports`@`localhost` |
+--------------------------------------------------+
  3. GRANT what ON where TO whom (2m). Write the four words on the board, then read the statement against them. 
    GRANT SELECT ON bookstore.* TO 'shop_reports'@'localhost';
SHOW GRANTS FOR 'shop_reports'@'localhost';
    +-------------------------------------------------------------+
| Grants for shop_reports@localhost                           |
+-------------------------------------------------------------+
| GRANT USAGE ON *.* TO `shop_reports`@`localhost`            |
| GRANT SELECT ON `bookstore`.* TO `shop_reports`@`localhost` |
+-------------------------------------------------------------+
    No FLUSH PRIVILEGES needed: grants take effect at once.
  4. The website's account, table by table (3m). "The site reads the catalog and records orders. It never changes a price or deletes anything." 
    CREATE USER 'shop_app'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED BY 'CHANGE-ME-app-1';
GRANT SELECT ON bookstore.* TO 'shop_app'@'localhost';
GRANT INSERT, UPDATE ON bookstore.customers   TO 'shop_app'@'localhost';
GRANT INSERT         ON bookstore.orders      TO 'shop_app'@'localhost';
GRANT INSERT         ON bookstore.order_items TO 'shop_app'@'localhost';
SHOW GRANTS FOR 'shop_app'@'localhost';
    +---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Grants for shop_app@localhost                                             |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| GRANT USAGE ON *.* TO `shop_app`@`localhost`                              |
| GRANT SELECT ON `bookstore`.* TO `shop_app`@`localhost`                   |
| GRANT INSERT, UPDATE ON `bookstore`.`customers` TO `shop_app`@`localhost` |
| GRANT INSERT ON `bookstore`.`order_items` TO `shop_app`@`localhost`       |
| GRANT INSERT ON `bookstore`.`orders` TO `shop_app`@`localhost`            |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------+
    If the site is ever broken into, the attacker can read the catalog and add orders, and nothing else.
  5. Column-level privileges (2m). "The support desk sees names and emails, and may fix an email. Nothing else." 
    CREATE USER 'support_desk'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED BY 'CHANGE-ME-support-1';
GRANT SELECT (id, first_name, last_name, email), UPDATE (email)
    ON bookstore.customers TO 'support_desk'@'localhost';
  6. Test as the user: shop_reports (4m). In the SECOND window: mysql -u shop_reports -p bookstore (the password is typed at the prompt, never on the command line). "A grant you have not tested is a guess: test one thing it must do and one it must not." 
    SELECT CURRENT_USER();
SELECT COUNT(*) AS books FROM books;
DELETE FROM books WHERE id = 11;
    +------------------------+
| CURRENT_USER()         |
+------------------------+
| shop_reports@localhost |
+------------------------+
+-------+
| books |
+-------+
|    11 |
+-------+
ERROR 1142 (42000): DELETE command denied to user 'shop_reports'@'localhost' for table 'books'
  7. Test as the user: support_desk (3m). exit, then mysql -u support_desk -p bookstore. Predict each line first. 
    SELECT first_name, email FROM customers WHERE id = 1;
SELECT first_name, city FROM customers WHERE id = 1;
SELECT * FROM orders;
    +------------+-------------------+
| first_name | email             |
+------------+-------------------+
| Alice      | alice@example.com |
+------------+-------------------+
ERROR 1143 (42000): SELECT command denied to user 'support_desk'@'localhost' for column 'city' in table 'customers'
ERROR 1142 (42000): SELECT command denied to user 'support_desk'@'localhost' for table 'orders'
    1143 names the column that was never granted; SELECT * would be refused for the whole table (1142).
  8. REVOKE at the level you granted (2m). Back in the root window. 
    REVOKE UPDATE ON bookstore.customers FROM 'shop_app'@'localhost';
SHOW GRANTS FOR 'shop_app'@'localhost';
    +---------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Grants for shop_app@localhost                                       |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
| GRANT USAGE ON *.* TO `shop_app`@`localhost`                        |
| GRANT SELECT ON `bookstore`.* TO `shop_app`@`localhost`             |
| GRANT INSERT ON `bookstore`.`customers` TO `shop_app`@`localhost`   |
| GRANT INSERT ON `bookstore`.`order_items` TO `shop_app`@`localhost` |
| GRANT INSERT ON `bookstore`.`orders` TO `shop_app`@`localhost`      |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
  9. The old advice, once more (1m). "Session 1 showed this error; here is where it comes from." 
    CREATE USER 'legacy_app'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED WITH mysql_native_password BY 'CHANGE-ME-legacy-1';
    ERROR 1524 (HY000): Plugin 'mysql_native_password' is not loaded
    Fix: update the app's driver, never weaken the server.
  10. Roles (3m). "Several clerks, same job: grant to a role once, then grant the role to people." 
    CREATE ROLE 'bookstore_reader', 'bookstore_sales';
GRANT SELECT ON bookstore.* TO 'bookstore_reader';
GRANT INSERT, UPDATE ON bookstore.orders      TO 'bookstore_sales';
GRANT INSERT, UPDATE ON bookstore.order_items TO 'bookstore_sales';
CREATE USER 'tom'@'localhost'  IDENTIFIED BY 'CHANGE-ME-tom-1';
CREATE USER 'lena'@'localhost' IDENTIFIED BY 'CHANGE-ME-lena-1';
GRANT 'bookstore_reader', 'bookstore_sales' TO 'tom'@'localhost', 'lena'@'localhost';
SHOW GRANTS FOR 'tom'@'localhost';
    +-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Grants for tom@localhost                                                |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| GRANT USAGE ON *.* TO `tom`@`localhost`                                 |
| GRANT `bookstore_reader`@`%`,`bookstore_sales`@`%` TO `tom`@`localhost` |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------+
  11. The step everybody forgets (4m). Second window, as tom WITHOUT a database: mysql -u tom -p. Then try mysql -u tom -p bookstore. 
    SELECT CURRENT_ROLE();
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM bookstore.books;
    +----------------+
| CURRENT_ROLE() |
+----------------+
| NONE           |
+----------------+
ERROR 1142 (42000): SELECT command denied to user 'tom'@'localhost' for table 'books'
ERROR 1044 (42000): Access denied for user 'tom'@'localhost' to database 'bookstore'
    The second line is the login with bookstore: refused before any query, because no privilege is active yet.
  12. SET DEFAULT ROLE (2m). Root window: 
    SET DEFAULT ROLE ALL TO 'tom'@'localhost', 'lena'@'localhost';
SELECT user, default_role_user FROM mysql.default_roles ORDER BY user, default_role_user;
    +------+-------------------+
| user | default_role_user |
+------+-------------------+
| lena | bookstore_reader  |
| lena | bookstore_sales   |
| tom  | bookstore_reader  |
| tom  | bookstore_sales   |
+------+-------------------+
  13. Tom, again (1m). Second window: mysql -u tom -p bookstore. 
    SELECT CURRENT_ROLE();
SELECT COUNT(*) AS books FROM books;
    +----------------------------------------------+
| CURRENT_ROLE()                               |
+----------------------------------------------+
| `bookstore_reader`@`%`,`bookstore_sales`@`%` |
+----------------------------------------------+
+-------+
| books |
+-------+
|    11 |
+-------+
  14. Lock an account (1m). "Staff on leave, a job you are retiring: lock it, keep its grants." Then try to log in as shop_reports. 
    ALTER USER 'shop_reports'@'localhost' ACCOUNT LOCK;
    ERROR 3118 (HY000): Access denied for user 'shop_reports'@'localhost'. Account is locked.
  15. Two refusals worth reading (1m). A misspelled account, then the right user with the wrong host. 
    GRANT SELECT ON bookstore.* TO 'reports'@'localhost';
SHOW GRANTS FOR 'shop_reports'@'%';
    ERROR 1410 (42000): You are not allowed to create a user with GRANT
ERROR 1141 (42000): There is no such grant defined for user 'shop_reports' on host '%'
  16. Clean up (1m). "Drop what you created; a demo account left on a server is a door left open." 
    DROP USER 'shop_reports'@'localhost', 'shop_app'@'localhost', 'support_desk'@'localhost',
          'tom'@'localhost', 'lena'@'localhost';
DROP ROLE 'bookstore_reader', 'bookstore_sales';
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • The right name, the wrong host. "It says access denied but I created the account!" Coach: SELECT user, host FROM mysql.user; and compare the pair; inside a session, SELECT USER(), CURRENT_USER();.
  • Granted but never activated. A role shows in SHOW GRANTS and the user still gets 1142 (or 1044 at login). Coach: "Is the role ACTIVE? SELECT CURRENT_ROLE(); as that user; the fix is SET DEFAULT ROLE ALL."
  • Revoking at another level. 1147 on a REVOKE. Coach: "Read SHOW GRANTS: at which level was it granted? Revoke exactly that line."
Guided lab

File: labs/module-12/l7_4-user-management/warehouse_accounts_starter.sql; reference labs/module-12/l7_4-user-management/warehouse_accounts_solution.sql. Two program accounts (the warehouse scanner, the accounting export) with table and column privileges, an audit through information_schema, a REVOKE and a lock. Every account is kit_... and the file drops them at the end.

  • TODO 2 together (4m), TODOs 3 and 5 in pairs (7m), TODO 6 if time (2m): the login test from a second window; the README has the steps.
  • Expected: the warehouse's grants merge into GRANT SELECT, UPDATE (in_stock) ON bookstore.books plus SELECT on authors; accounting gets three customer columns, orders and order_items; the audit lists 4 table and 4 column privileges; after TODO 5 accounting keeps customers and orders and kit_warehouse is locked (Y).
  • TODO 6, as kit_warehouse (real output): the in_stock UPDATE works, the price UPDATE is refused:
Query OK, 1 row affected
Rows matched: 1  Changed: 1  Warnings: 0
ERROR 1143 (42000): UPDATE command denied to user 'kit_warehouse'@'localhost' for column 'price' in table 'books'
  • Circulate for: column lists in the wrong place (GRANT SELECT ON customers (id, ...): ERROR 1064; the columns go after the privilege), a REVOKE that names bookstore.* instead of the table, and anyone typing a statement aimed at root.
  • Fast finishers: labs/module-12/l7_4-user-management/staff_roles_starter.sql (roles: grant, activate, change once for everybody, revoke a bundle), then labs/module-12/l7_4-user-management/debug_grants.sql (errors 1410, 1141, 1396 and 1147).
Start the lesson project

The lesson project is Accounts for the Bookstore Team: team_access.sql, a rerunnable script that creates three roles and four accounts. In the room (10 minutes): Steps 1 to 3.

  • Step 1: the DROP USER IF EXISTS / DROP ROLE IF EXISTS lines that make the script rerunnable. Step 2: the three roles. Step 3: the website account, table by table.
  • Done in the room = the file runs twice in a row without an error, and SHOW GRANTS for team_web lists four SELECTs and two INSERTs.
  • Goes home: Steps 4 to 6 (people and the dashboard with default roles, the inventory query, the two-direction test as team_sofia). Remind learners to set Dune back to 14.99 after Step 6.
Checks for understanding

Ask these out loud during the demo and the lab:

  • What can an account do right after CREATE USER? Answer: connect, nothing else (USAGE).
  • 1142 or 1143: which names a column? Answer: 1143.
  • A role is granted and the user still gets 1142. What is missing? Answer: an active role: SET DEFAULT ROLE ALL (or SET ROLE in the session).
  • Why does the website get no DELETE anywhere? Answer: least privilege: a bug or an attacker using its login can do only what it needs.

Workbook self-check, items Q1 to Q6.

Answers: Q1 B) Two separate accounts, each with its own password and privileges; Q2 Three: GRANT USAGE ON *.*, GRANT SELECT ON `bookstore`.*, GRANT INSERT ON `bookstore`.`orders`; Q3 D) The account has column privileges on customers, and city is not one of them; Q4 A) Right away, both of them, and every future account given the role; Q5 C) Fails with ERROR 1147: there is no table-level grant on books to take away; Q6 ALTER USER 'kit_mailer'@'localhost' ACCOUNT LOCK;. Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: two windows can overwhelm; pair learners so one types as root and the other logs in as the demo account, then swap. Keep the error ladder on the board.

Stretch: staff_roles_starter.sql and debug_grants.sql; the lesson's Exercise 3 (a marketing view granted instead of columns) and Exercise 4 (a remote account with REQUIRE SSL and a query limit), then the Bonus Challenge (the exact privileges mysqldump needs: it leads straight into 8.1).

8.1

Importing & Exporting Data

65 min
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Back up a database with mysqldump, choose the right options for MySQL 9.7, and restore the file into a test database
  • Explain what secure_file_priv and local_infile control, and find their values on your server
  • Export query results to a CSV file with SELECT ... INTO OUTFILE, including a header row
  • Import a CSV file through a staging table with LOAD DATA INFILE, converting dates and empty values on the way in
  • Recognize and fix the errors file operations raise (1290, 1086, 3948, 2068, 3546)
Timing in the room
10mFrame: whole databases at the terminal, rows at mysql>; where the server may touch files
30mLive demo: backup, test restore, the GTID trap; a CSV export; a staging-table import; refusals and a silent warning
15mGuided lab: new_titles_import_starter.sql in pairs
10mStart the lesson project: the backup kit, Steps 1 and 2
Mental model to say

"Two kinds of moving data, two kinds of tools." Point at the board: whole databases (backups, a new server) go through mysqldump, a program at the terminal, which writes a .sql file of CREATE and INSERT statements; the mysql client plays it back. Rows (a report for a spreadsheet, a supplier's file) go through SELECT ... INTO OUTFILE and LOAD DATA, SQL at the mysql> prompt.

Then the fence: "INTO OUTFILE and LOAD DATA INFILE run on the SERVER's disk, so the server only touches one folder, secure_file_priv, and only for accounts with the FILE privilege. LOCAL means the client sends a file from your own computer, and both sides must agree to it."

Then the rule that makes a backup a backup: a dump you have never restored is a hope. Back up, restore into a test database, compare the counts, write it down.

Live demo (you type, they follow)

From a fresh bookstore.sql, with three windows: a plain terminal in the course folder (mysqldump), the root mysql client, and the lesson page for the CSV files.

  1. A backup, at the terminal (3m). Open a plain terminal (not mysql>) in the course folder. "mysqldump is a separate program. On 9.7 these four options are the habit." 
    mysqldump -u root -p --single-transaction --routines --events \
    --set-gtid-purged=OFF bookstore > bookstore_backup.sql
    It prints nothing and leaves a file of about 12 KB. Windows PowerShell: --result-file=bookstore_backup.sql instead of >.
  2. What is inside (2m). "Plain SQL: a DROP and a CREATE per table, then the rows." 
    grep -c 'INSERT INTO' bookstore_backup.sql
grep 'DROP TABLE IF EXISTS' bookstore_backup.sql
    8
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `authors`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `book_categories`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `books`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `categories`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `customers`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `employees`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `order_items`;
DROP TABLE IF EXISTS `orders`;
    Every table starts with DROP TABLE IF EXISTS: a restore replaces tables. Open the file in an editor for the versioned /*!40101 ... */ comments.
  3. A test restore (3m). "A backup you have never restored is a hope." Restore into a test database, then compare. 
    mysql -u root -p -e "CREATE DATABASE bookstore_test"
mysql -u root -p bookstore_test < bookstore_backup.sql
mysql -u root -p -t -e "SELECT
    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM bookstore.order_items) AS items_original,
    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM bookstore_test.order_items) AS items_copy"
    +----------------+------------+
| items_original | items_copy |
+----------------+------------+
|             18 |         18 |
+----------------+------------+
    Then DROP DATABASE bookstore_test;.
  4. The 9.7 trap (3m). "Leave out --set-gtid-purged=OFF and watch." 
    mysqldump -u root -p --single-transaction bookstore > plain_dump.sql
mysql -u root -p -e "CREATE DATABASE bookstore_test2"
mysql -u root -p bookstore_test2 < plain_dump.sql
    Warning: A partial dump from a server that has GTIDs will by default include the GTIDs of all transactions, even those that changed suppressed parts of the database. If you don't want to restore GTIDs, pass --set-gtid-purged=OFF. To make a complete dump, pass --all-databases --triggers --routines --events.
ERROR 3546 (HY000) at line 24: @@GLOBAL.GTID_PURGED cannot be changed: the added gtid set must not overlap with @@GLOBAL.GTID_EXECUTED
    9.7 starts with GTIDs on: the plain dump carries the server's replication history and refuses to restore on the same server. No tables were created. Take the dump again with the option.
  5. And at the wrong prompt (1m). Back in the mysql client, the classic slip: 
    mysqldump -u root -p bookstore > bookstore_backup.sql;
    ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax; check the manual that corresponds to your MySQL server version for the right syntax to use near 'mysqldump -u root -p bookstore > bookstore_backup.sql' at line 1
    Type exit, run it at $ or PS>.
  6. Where the server may write files (2m). "INTO OUTFILE and LOAD DATA INFILE run on the SERVER's disk, inside one folder." On a Linux package or the Docker image (shown: MySQL 8.4's Docker image): 
    SHOW VARIABLES LIKE 'secure_file_priv';
    +------------------+-----------------------+
| Variable_name    | Value                 |
+------------------+-----------------------+
| secure_file_priv | /var/lib/mysql-files/ |
+------------------+-----------------------+
    Learners run it on their own server and write the answer down: every path below uses that folder. (The kit's test server keeps its folder elsewhere; its outputs below are real, with the path shown as /var/lib/mysql-files/.)
  7. Export a CSV (3m). "Any query, plus INTO OUTFILE and the format, at the end." 
    SELECT b.id, b.title, a.name AS author, b.price, b.published
FROM books AS b
JOIN authors AS a ON a.id = b.author_id
ORDER BY b.id
INTO OUTFILE '/var/lib/mysql-files/books_export.csv'
    FIELDS TERMINATED BY ',' OPTIONALLY ENCLOSED BY '"'
    LINES TERMINATED BY '\n';
    Query OK, 11 rows affected
    The first lines of the file (open it with a text editor or a spreadsheet; on Linux it belongs to the mysql user): 
    1,"Dune","Frank Herbert",14.99,"1965-08-01"
2,"1984","George Orwell",9.99,"1949-06-08"
3,"To Kill a Mockingbird","Harper Lee",11.99,"1960-07-11"
...
  8. Outside the folder (1m). "Anywhere else is refused." 
    SELECT title, price FROM books
INTO OUTFILE '/tmp/books.csv';
    ERROR 1290 (HY000): The MySQL server is running with the --secure-file-priv option so it cannot execute this statement
  9. Import through a staging table (4m). Show new_books.csv first (on the lesson page): a header, a comma inside "I, Robot", US dates, an empty page count, and an author the store does not know. "Never load a stranger's file straight into a real table." 
    CREATE TABLE import_books (
    title     VARCHAR(200),
    author    VARCHAR(100),
    price     DECIMAL(8, 2),
    pages     INT,
    published DATE
);
LOAD DATA INFILE '/var/lib/mysql-files/new_books.csv'
INTO TABLE import_books
    FIELDS TERMINATED BY ',' OPTIONALLY ENCLOSED BY '"'
    LINES TERMINATED BY '\n'
    IGNORE 1 LINES
    (title, author, price, @pages, @published)
SET pages     = NULLIF(@pages, ''),
    published = STR_TO_DATE(@published, '%m/%d/%Y');
    Query OK, 0 rows affected
Query OK, 4 rows affected
Records: 4  Deleted: 0  Skipped: 0  Warnings: 0
    Read the status line aloud: 4 records, 0 skipped, 0 warnings.
  10. Look at what arrived (1m). 
    SELECT * FROM import_books;
    +------------------+----------------+-------+-------+------------+
| title            | author         | price | pages | published  |
+------------------+----------------+-------+-------+------------+
| I, Robot         | Isaac Asimov   |  8.99 |   253 | 1950-12-02 |
| Emma             | Jane Austen    |  8.49 |   474 | 1815-12-23 |
| Frankenstein     | Mary Shelley   |  7.49 |  NULL | 1818-01-01 |
| The Silmarillion | J.R.R. Tolkien | 12.99 |   365 | 1977-09-15 |
+------------------+----------------+-------+-------+------------+
  11. Check against the real tables (2m). "Which authors are missing? A LEFT JOIN with IS NULL." 
    SELECT i.title, i.author
FROM import_books AS i
LEFT JOIN authors AS a ON a.name = i.author
WHERE a.id IS NULL;
    +--------------+--------------+
| title        | author       |
+--------------+--------------+
| Frankenstein | Mary Shelley |
+--------------+--------------+
  12. Copy the good rows, all or nothing (3m). "Lesson 7.2 comes back: one transaction." 
    INSERT INTO authors (name, birth_year) VALUES ('Mary Shelley', 1797);
START TRANSACTION;
INSERT INTO books (title, author_id, price, pages, published)
SELECT i.title, a.id, i.price, i.pages, i.published
FROM import_books AS i
JOIN authors AS a ON a.name = i.author
ORDER BY i.title;
SELECT b.id, b.title, a.name AS author, b.pages, b.published
FROM books AS b
JOIN authors AS a ON a.id = b.author_id
WHERE b.id > 11
ORDER BY b.id;
COMMIT;
DROP TABLE import_books;
    +----+------------------+----------------+-------+------------+
| id | title            | author         | pages | published  |
+----+------------------+----------------+-------+------------+
| 12 | Emma             | Jane Austen    |   474 | 1815-12-23 |
| 13 | Frankenstein     | Mary Shelley   |  NULL | 1818-01-01 |
| 14 | I, Robot         | Isaac Asimov   |   253 | 1950-12-02 |
| 15 | The Silmarillion | J.R.R. Tolkien |   365 | 1977-09-15 |
+----+------------------+----------------+-------+------------+
  13. The silent LOCAL import (2m). "With LOCAL the client sends a file from your own computer, and bad values become warnings, not errors." (local_infile is ON on the demo server; Lesson 8.1's Common Mistake 4 shows the 3948 error when it is OFF.) 
    CREATE TABLE price_update (title VARCHAR(200), price DECIMAL(8, 2));
LOAD DATA LOCAL INFILE 'price_update.csv'
INTO TABLE price_update
    FIELDS TERMINATED BY ','
    IGNORE 1 LINES;
SHOW WARNINGS;
    Query OK, 0 rows affected
Query OK, 2 rows affected, 1 warning
Records: 2  Deleted: 0  Skipped: 0  Warnings: 1
+---------+------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| Level   | Code | Message                                                    |
+---------+------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| Warning | 1366 | Incorrect decimal value: 'N/A' for column 'price' at row 2 |
+---------+------+------------------------------------------------------------+
    Emma now costs 0.00 and the only clue was Warnings: 1. Read the warnings right after every import (the next statement clears them).
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • The wrong prompt. mysqldump at mysql> (1064) or SQL at $ ("command not found"). Coach: "Look at the prompt first: $/PS> runs programs, mysql> runs SQL."
  • A path the server cannot use. 1290 for /tmp or a home folder. Coach: "Whose disk is that path on? The server's. Which folder does it allow?" SHOW VARIABLES LIKE 'secure_file_priv';
  • Trusting the status line's first number. "4 rows affected, done." Coach: read the whole line (Skipped, Warnings), SHOW WARNINGS when it is not 0, and SELECT the staging table before copying anything.
Guided lab

File: labs/module-12/l8_1-importing-exporting/new_titles_import_starter.sql (and labs/module-12/l8_1-importing-exporting/new_titles.csv, the supplier's file); reference labs/module-12/l8_1-importing-exporting/new_titles_import_solution.sql. A European supplier's file (semicolons, day.month.year, an N/A price, an empty page count, a new author, a book already sold) through a staging table. The staging table is filled with an INSERT of exactly the text LOAD DATA would store, so the lab needs no file access or server setting.

  • SOURCE it as shipped (2m): The Two Towers costs 0.00 and every date is NULL, with a screen of warnings (1411, 1292). Ask: "Which of these would have reached the books table?"
  • TODO 1 together (4m), TODOs 2 and 3 in pairs (7m). TODO 3 is a commented block: learners remove the -- and put in the date pattern from TODO 1.
  • Expected: after TODO 1, no warnings and real dates; Step 2 holds back Animal Farm (already sold) and The Two Towers (no price) and shows Bram Stoker's author_id as NULL (new author); after TODO 3, books 12 Dracula, 13 Foundation, 14 Persuasion (pages NULL); 14 books, 11 authors.
  • Real file, optional (README): with local_infile on, the LOAD DATA LOCAL statement in the lab's header loads new_titles.csv into the same staging table (real output, the kit's test server):
+----------------+----------------+-----------+-----------+---------------+
| title          | author         | price_raw | pages_raw | published_raw |
+----------------+----------------+-----------+-----------+---------------+
| Animal Farm    | George Orwell  | 8.49      | 112       | 17.08.1945    |
| Dracula        | Bram Stoker    | 8.99      | 418       | 26.05.1897    |
| Foundation     | Isaac Asimov   | 9.99      | 255       | 01.06.1951    |
| Persuasion     | Jane Austen    | 7.49      |           | 20.12.1817    |
| The Two Towers | J.R.R. Tolkien | N/A       | 352       | 11.11.1954    |
+----------------+----------------+-----------+-----------+---------------+
  • Fast finishers: labs/module-12/l8_1-importing-exporting/backup_check_starter.sql (a least-privilege backup account and a proof it cannot write, the restore test's reference counts, a CSV-shaped report with a header row), then labs/module-12/l8_1-importing-exporting/debug_import.sql (errors 1064, 1290, 1411 and 1292).
Start the lesson project

The lesson project is The Bookstore Backup Kit: a backup account, a tested dump, a monthly sales CSV and a repeatable import. In the room (10 minutes): Steps 1 and 2.

  • Step 1: backup_user.sql (reload, CREATE USER IF NOT EXISTS bookstore_backup, the five privileges, SHOW GRANTS).
  • Step 2: at the terminal, the dump AS bookstore_backup with --no-tablespaces --skip-masking-policies (on 8.4 without the last option).
  • Done in the room = a dated bookstore_YYYY-MM-DD.sql made by the backup account, with no warnings on screen.
  • Goes home: Steps 3 to 6 (the test restore and restore_log.txt, the sales CSV checked by loading it back, the repeatable import, the scheduled backup with a login path).
Checks for understanding

Ask these out loud during the demo and the lab:

  • mysqldump: terminal or mysql>? Answer: terminal.
  • Which option does every 9.7 dump you restore yourself need? Answer: --set-gtid-purged=OFF.
  • What does a database dump leave out that the server needs? Answer: the user accounts and their grants (keep team_access.sql and backup_user.sql).
  • Why a staging table? Answer: to look, convert and check before the real tables change.

Workbook self-check, items Q7 to Q12.

Answers: Q7 C) At the terminal's shell prompt ($ or PS>), not inside the mysql client; Q8 A) --routines; Q9 1976-07-04 and NULL; Q10 D) Name the target database: mysql -u root -p bookstore_test < bookstore_backup.sql (or dump with --databases); Q11 B) To look at, convert and check the rows (dates, empty values, missing authors) before any real table changes; Q12 C) ERROR 1086: the file already exists. Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: learners on Windows do the shell part with --result-file and the --commands -e "SOURCE ..." restore (the README spells it out); learners without file access skip the server-side export and use the lab's on-screen report.

Stretch: backup_check_starter.sql and debug_import.sql; the lesson's Exercise 2 (signups with IGNORE and NULLIF) and Exercise 4 (a round trip that proves itself), then the Bonus Challenge (mysqldump --tab).

Final Project Brief (hand-out)

2:30

Final Project Brief: the Maple Street Tool Library

20 min
Timing
3mHand out the brief: Lesson 8.2's "The Brief" and "What you hand in" (lesson_final_project.html)
4mRead Rosa's needs aloud; learners underline nouns (tables) and circle rules
6mIn pairs: a first list of entities and relationships; one minute of sharing on the board
5mThe eight milestones, the rubric (How Your Work Is Assessed, 100 points), the two studio sessions
2mQuestions; the homework: the Milestone 1 draft
What to say and show
  • "Until now the bookstore was handed to you. For the last two sessions you start from a client's description and an empty server." The client is Rosa Delgado, who coordinates a volunteer tool library: two people borrowed the same ladder, nobody knows who has the drill, someone took the chainsaw without the course.
  • Read the brief's eight bullet points aloud. Learners underline the nouns (members, tools, categories, safety courses, loans) and circle the rules (one open loan per tool, nothing out while in repair, suspended members cannot borrow, at most three tools at once, no course no chainsaw).
  • Pairs, 5 minutes: which nouns become tables, which become columns, which relationships are one-to-many and which many-to-many? Collect on the board: five entities plus the junction between members and safety courses (it carries the completion date). Do not show the lesson's reference design: it is hidden on the page for a reason.
  • Show "What you hand in" (01_schema.sql to 05_security.sql, a tested backup, README.md) and the eight milestones with their lessons: every Part 2 lesson comes back (5.1 and 5.2 in the Saturday queries, 6.2 and 7.1 and 7.2 in the business logic, 7.4 in security, 8.1 in the backup). Show the rubric: 80 of 100 is a solid, portfolio-ready project.
  • Sessions 13 and 14 are studios: the room is for building, with you circulating. "Arrive at Session 13 with the brief read and your Milestone 1 draft: you will turn it into tables in the first hour."
Instructor notes
  • Mark the final project with the kit's final-assessment.html (rubric and instructor notes with the reference numbers); learners use the lesson page's rubric. Keep the kit file out of the learners' folder.
  • Learners who prefer another business can use the lesson's alternative brief (a small clinic, in the page's Common Questions). Agree on it now, not in Session 13.
  • Everyone needs MySQL 9.7 with room for a new database, tool_library; the bookstore is not touched. Learners who miss Session 13 can still start from the lesson page alone.

Closing

Exit ticket (3 questions, no notes, 4 minutes)
  1. Why does the website's account get INSERT on orders but no UPDATE or DELETE anywhere? Answer: least privilege: a bug or an attacker using the website's login can only do what the website needs (read the catalog, add orders).
  2. Name the two steps that turn a dump file into a backup you can trust. Answer: dump with the right options (on 9.7 with --set-gtid-purged=OFF), then restore it into a test database and compare counts (and write it down).
  3. Who is the final project's client, and name two rules her database must enforce. Answer: Rosa Delgado of the Maple Street Tool Library; any two of: one open loan per tool, nothing out while in repair, suspended members cannot borrow, at most three tools per member, no safety course no chainsaw.
Homework (about 2 to 3 hours)
  • Finish both lesson projects. team_access.sql Steps 4 to 6 (default roles, the inventory, the test as team_sofia) and the backup kit Steps 3 to 6 (test restore and restore_log.txt, the sales CSV, the repeatable import, backup.sh).
  • Lesson exercises. 7.4: Exercises 1 to 3; Exercise 4 is a stretch. 8.1: Exercises 1 to 3; Exercise 4 is a stretch.
  • Both Quick Quizzes on the lesson pages, and both journal entries.
  • Labs not reached in class: staff_roles_starter.sql, debug_grants.sql, backup_check_starter.sql, debug_import.sql.
  • Final project, Milestone 1 draft (about 30 minutes): read the whole brief and the lesson's "From Requirements to Tables" section; on paper, the entities and their columns, the relationships (one or many, both directions), and where each of Rosa's five rules will be enforced (column type or constraint, foreign key, or procedure). No SQL yet.
  • Before the Next Lesson (from 8.1): skim Lessons 6.3 (design) and 7.4 (users): the final project leans on both.
Bring list for Session 13
  • A laptop with MySQL 9.7 and the root password; room for a new database (tool_library).
  • The Milestone 1 draft (entities, relationships, where each rule lives) and the brief.
  • team_access.sql and the backup kit scripts: the final project's security and backup milestones reuse them.
  • The workbook and the journal; the course folder with labs/.

If You Run Long or Short

Running long: what to protect and what to cut first
  • Protect: the logins as another account; the test restore and the GTID trap; the staging-table import; the full 20 minutes of the brief.
  • Cut first (saves 2m): the 8.1 LOCAL warning step.
  • Cut second (saves 3m): the 7.4 old-advice and two-refusals steps (debug_grants.sql has the refusals).
  • Cut third (saves 10m): the 8.1 project start: Step 1 is three statements learners run at home.
Running short: what to add
  • The 7.4 Bonus Challenge live: a team_backup account with exactly mysqldump's privileges, then a dump made with it at the terminal.
  • Run debug_import.sql for everyone and fix it together.
  • Start Milestone 1 in the room: pairs compare their entity lists.
Instructor Kit · Facilitator Guide

Session 13: Final Project Kickoff and Studio I

How to run the first final-project studio. Launch the Maple Street Tool Library brief and its rubric, show the build mistakes that cost the most time, and get every learner from Rosa's eight requirements to a checked design, a re-runnable schema whose constraints are proven by failing tests, and test data that plants the edge cases.

1 lesson (first half) 3 hrs session Lesson 8.2 · Milestones 1–3 Session 13 of 14 · Part 2

How to Use This Guide

  • Session 13 is a studio: one lesson (8.2, the final project) and most of the room's time spent building. The shape: warm-up → kickoff (the brief, the files, the rubric) → a short frame and live demo → Milestone 1 in the room → a guided lab on the bookstore → Milestones 2 and 3 in the room. You circulate and run short check-ins at the end of each milestone instead of teaching new material.
  • Type, do not paste, in the demo. Every SQL block below was run to produce the output printed under it, on MySQL 9.7 (the lesson's own blocks were run as one session, exactly as the page shows them, and match the page). Read error numbers aloud from the left.
  • The lesson page is the brief and the reference. Keep lesson_final_project.html open in a tab: The Brief, Milestones and Timeline, From Requirements to Tables, How Your Work Is Assessed, Common Mistakes. Do not open the reference solutions on the projector before learners have attempted a milestone; the page hides them on purpose.
  • The assessment, the rubric with scoring guidance and the instructor answer notes with real reference numbers are in final-assessment.html at the kit root (Part 2 section). Print the rubric for learners from there.
  • Session 14 continues the same project (Milestones 5 to 7 in the room, then the showcase and Lesson 8.3). Teal blocks are hands-on, amber blocks are watch-outs.
The #1 risk of Session 13: a design nobody checked

A learner who starts typing CREATE TABLE before every requirement has a home spends Session 14 rebuilding. The two most expensive design errors are (1) trying to put a rule that looks at other rows into a CHECK (it cannot work, and the learner discovers that at Milestone 5) and (2) storing derived data (an is_available or times_borrowed column) that the queries then trust. The defense: nobody starts Milestone 2 without a passed design check-in (two minutes: point at each of Rosa's eight requirements and say where it lives). Keep a tally on the studio tracker below; visit the learners without a tick first.

Before the Session

  • ☐ Check what Session 12 handed out: the brief (the lesson page's "The Brief" section, or the printed one-pager) and the reading for this session (the brief and "From Requirements to Tables"). Learners who did not read it can still do Milestone 1 in the room; it takes them ten minutes longer.
  • ☐ Print or share: the rubric and the milestone checklist (from final-assessment.html, Part 2), the workbook (its Milestone tracker), and sticky notes for the check-ins. Optional: A3 paper for design diagrams.
  • ☐ Files to hand out: labs/module-13/ without the *_solution.sql files. Run python3 labs/check_labs.py module-13 once (6 files, every line PASS, a few seconds).
  • ☐ Projector machine: MySQL 9.7 with the bookstore loaded (the guided lab uses it: SOURCE labs/_states/bookstore.sql), a terminal in the course folder at 18 pt, a second terminal inside an empty tool-library folder (the demo of where to start the client), browser tabs lesson_final_project.html and final-assessment.html.
  • ☐ Board, before learners arrive: a three-column table headed "Rosa's requirement · Where it lives · Why", with rows 1 to 8 and the middle column empty; the four error numbers 1064 · 3780 · 3814 · 1824; and "Rules about ONE row → constraint · rules about OTHER rows → procedure + transaction".
  • ☐ Decide the alternative-brief policy: the lesson's FAQ allows another business (at least five tables, a many-to-many relationship, a rule across rows, two kinds of users; the clinic brief is ready-made). Approve own briefs in writing today; they cannot use the reference numbers in final-assessment.html, so they write their own expected results.
Known traps in this session
SymptomCauseFix
SOURCE 01_schema.sql inside 02_data.sql: Failed to open file '01_schema.sql', error: 2SOURCE paths are relative to the folder the CLIENT was started in, not to the file that contains the SOURCE line (measured)Start the client inside the project folder: cd tool-library, then mysql -u root -p. Or write the path from the course folder in both places.
ERROR 1064 ... near 'conditionA reserved word as a column nameRename it (tool_condition); not backticks.
ERROR 3780 ... are incompatibleINT versus INT UNSIGNED or BIGINT on the two sides of a foreign keyOne key type everywhere (INT).
ERROR 1824 Failed to open the referenced tableChild table created before its parentReorder: lookup tables, then the tables that point at them, junction and history last.
ERROR 3814 ... contains disallowed function: curdateA time rule written as a CHECKCompare two columns of the row; time rules go in the procedure.
Second run of 01_schema.sql fails with ERROR 1050 Table ... already existsThe script has no DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS first lineAdd it; rerun the whole file.
The data script stops halfway: ERROR 1452A loan or course record points at an id that was not inserted (or inserted later)Explicit ids, parents' INSERTs first (members, categories, courses, tools, then member_courses and loans).
Someone's dates fail chk_loans_due (3819)due_on typed before loaned_on, or the two dates swapped in the VALUES listRead the column list of the INSERT against the VALUES; the CHECK just caught a real data error.

In the room. The lesson estimates 240 minutes of focused work for eight milestones (30 + 40 + 25 + 45 + 40 + 20 + 20 + 20). This session gives the room Milestones 1 to 3 (95 minutes of the lesson's estimate) plus the kickoff, a demo and a guided lab on the bookstore. Session 14 takes Milestones 5 to 7 in the room. Milestone 4 (the Saturday queries) and Milestone 8 (self-review and the README) are homework, because they are individual, quiet work that does not need the room.

Goes home (about 3 to 5 hours). Finish Milestones 1 to 3 if the room did not; Milestone 4 (all six Saturday queries with @today = '2026-06-15', two checked by hand); the edge-cases lab (edge_cases_starter.sql), which practices exactly the patterns Milestone 4 needs; the journal entry. The lesson's Quick Quiz and Exercises wait until after Milestone 5 (they run on the database as Milestone 5 leaves it).

Warm-up and Kickoff

Warm-up questions (answers in the Answer Key)

Learners answer on the workbook's warm-up page (5 minutes), then you take answers by cold call. W1 to W4 are the Session 12 lessons, which the project uses in Milestones 6 and 7; W5 checks the reading.

W1
The treasurer's account was granted the reporting role, but every SELECT gives ERROR 1142. What was forgotten? (Lesson 7.4)
W2
Someone types mysqldump -u root -p bookstore > backup.sql at the mysql> prompt. What happens? (Lesson 8.1)
W3

Sequence and talking points
  • Warm-up debrief (5m). For each question, one learner answers, one adds. On W5 write the six table names on the board; if nobody names member_courses, ask: "a member finishes many courses, a course is finished by many members: where does the date go?" Then: "hands up if you have a design draft from the reading." Note the hands: those learners do the Milestone 1 check-in first.
  • The brief (5m). Read Rosa's eight requirements aloud from the lesson page while learners follow in the workbook. Say: "This is a real kind of client: she describes problems, not tables. Your job is to turn eight sentences into six tables, a view, two procedures, two roles and a backup." Point at requirement 5: "five rules in one sentence; most of the project's thinking is here."
  • The deliverables (3m). Show "What you hand in": 01_schema.sql, 02_data.sql, 03_queries.sql, 04_logic.sql, 05_security.sql, a tested backup and README.md. Say: "Numbered scripts, each one runs with SOURCE, and 02_data.sql runs 01_schema.sql first, so the whole database rebuilds from files. That is what makes it portfolio-ready."
  • The rubric (4m). Hand out the printed rubric (seven areas, 100 points; 0, half or full points per area; 80 or more is solid). Say: "Notice what earns full points: proof. A constraint is proven when a statement that breaks it is refused. A query is checked when you compared it with the data by hand." Then the timeline: Milestones 1 to 3 today, 4 at home, 5 to 7 in Session 14, 8 and the README at home; submission one week after Session 14.
  • The folder (3m). On the projector: make a tool-library folder next to labs/, cd into it, start the client THERE. Say: "02_data.sql says SOURCE 01_schema.sql. SOURCE looks in the folder the client was started in, not next to the file. Start the client inside tool-library and it just works." (Measured on 9.7; the lesson page does not say it.)
Watch-outs
  • "Can I look at the reference?" Yes, after trying: the lesson says so. Ask them to try for at least the milestone's time first, then compare and write one difference into their design notes. Copying the reference wholesale scores poorly on Design ("every rule mapped ... with reasons") because the reasons are missing.
  • Own briefs need your written approval today (see Before the Session). Hold them to the same structure: five tables, a many-to-many, a rule across rows, two kinds of users.
  • Learners who missed Session 12 have not seen roles (7.4) or mysqldump (8.1). They can do all of today; send them to Lessons 7.4 and 8.1 before Session 14.

Lesson Plan

8.2

Final Project: Design and Build a Database

140 min (of 240)
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Turn a written brief into entities, relationships and business rules, and draw them as a diagram
  • Build a normalized schema in a re-runnable script, with keys, foreign keys and CHECK constraints that you have tested
  • Answer business questions with joins, GROUP BY, CTEs and window functions, using a fixed report date
  • Enforce rules that span several rows in a stored procedure with a transaction
  • Secure the database with roles and least-privilege accounts, then back it up and prove the backup restores

Today covers the first two objectives in the room (design, schema with tested constraints) and starts the third at home (the Saturday queries). Session 14 covers the last two.

Timing in the room
20mFrame and live demo: from requirements to tables; four build mistakes and the silent LEFT JOIN
30mStudio: Milestone 1, design, with a check-in per learner
15mGuided lab: gift_cards_starter.sql, then debug_gift_card_proofs.sql
40mStudio: Milestone 2, 01_schema.sql and its constraint tests (5-minute mini-demo of the test pattern first)
25mStudio: Milestone 3, 02_data.sql with the edge cases
10m(in the wrap-up) Milestone 4 explained for home
Mental model to say (5 minutes)

Use the lesson's four moves, pointing at the board's empty table: "Underline the nouns. Things with their own facts become tables; facts about a thing become columns. Read the verbs and ask 'one or many?' in both directions: one-to-many puts the foreign key on the many side; many-to-many needs a junction table, and here the junction carries its own fact, the completion date."

Then the move that decides the project: "Sort the rules by where they can live. A rule about ONE value, a type or a constraint: NOT NULL, UNIQUE, ENUM, CHECK. A rule BETWEEN tables, a foreign key. A rule that has to LOOK AT OTHER ROWS (only one open loan per tool, at most three per member) can never be a CHECK: it goes in the procedure that creates loans, inside a transaction." Fill in two rows of the board table together (requirement 2: tools columns, ENUM, CHECK > 0; requirement 5: checkout_tool), then leave the rest for the studio.

Last: "Leave out what can be calculated. Overdue, days late, available: they depend on today or on other rows, so they are computed in queries, never stored."

Live demo (you type, they follow; 15 minutes)

The lesson's Common Mistakes, typed in the mysql> client (any folder; the scratch database is dropped at the end). Ask for a prediction before each block. The outputs are the real ones; they match the lesson page.

  1. A scratch database for the mistakes (1m). "Before you build Rosa's database, five mistakes that cost the most time in projects like this. I make them in a scratch database so nothing collides with your project." 
    CREATE DATABASE pitfall_demo;
USE pitfall_demo;
    Database changed
  2. Mistake 1: a reserved word (2m). Ask first: "tool condition is in the brief. What could go wrong with this column?" Then run it. 
    CREATE TABLE demo_tools (
    id        INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    condition VARCHAR(10)
);
    ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax; check the manual that corresponds to your MySQL server version for the right syntax to use near 'condition VARCHAR(10)
)' at line 3
    CONDITION is reserved (stored programs use it). Read where the parser stopped: near 'condition. Fix: tool_condition. Same trap: order, group, rank, key.
  3. Mistake 2: a foreign key whose type does not match (3m). "A parent table, then a loans table whose tool_id is BIGINT." 
    CREATE TABLE demo_tools (id INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY, name VARCHAR(50));

CREATE TABLE demo_loans (
    id      INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    tool_id BIGINT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (tool_id) REFERENCES demo_tools (id)
);
    ERROR 3780 (HY000): Referencing column 'tool_id' and referenced column 'id' in foreign key constraint 'demo_loans_ibfk_1' are incompatible.
    INT and BIGINT clash; so do INT and INT UNSIGNED. Decide one key type now and write it in the design notes.
  4. Mistake 3: today's date in a CHECK (2m). "Due dates must be in the future. Sounds like a CHECK, right?" Let them say yes, then run it. 
    CREATE TABLE demo_loans (
    id     INT AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    due_on DATE NOT NULL,
    CONSTRAINT chk_due_future CHECK (due_on >= CURDATE())
);
    ERROR 3814 (HY000): An expression of a check constraint 'chk_due_future' contains disallowed function: curdate.
    A CHECK must give the same answer for a row forever. Compare columns of the same row (due_on >= loaned_on) and put time rules in the procedure.
  5. Mistake 4: the child before the parent (2m). "The course records table, written before the members table." 
    CREATE TABLE demo_courses_done (
    member_id INT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (member_id) REFERENCES demo_members (id)
);
    ERROR 1824 (HY000): Failed to open the referenced table 'demo_members'
    Parents first, junction and history tables last; drop in the reverse order.
  6. Mistake 5: the silent one (4m). "June loans per member, members with none should show 0." Ask for a prediction: how many rows? Most say three. 
    CREATE TABLE demo_members (id INT PRIMARY KEY, name VARCHAR(50));
CREATE TABLE demo_member_loans (member_id INT, loaned_on DATE);
INSERT INTO demo_members VALUES (1, 'Rosa'), (2, 'Kevin'), (3, 'Tessa');
INSERT INTO demo_member_loans VALUES (1, '2026-06-06'), (2, '2026-05-02');

SELECT m.name, COUNT(l.member_id) AS june_loans
FROM demo_members AS m
LEFT JOIN demo_member_loans AS l ON l.member_id = m.id
WHERE l.loaned_on >= '2026-06-01'
GROUP BY m.id, m.name
ORDER BY m.name;
    +------+------------+
| name | june_loans |
+------+------------+
| Rosa |          1 |
+------+------------+
    Kevin and Tessa vanished, with no error. WHERE runs after the join and throws away the NULL rows. This is the mistake the rubric's Queries row is most often lost on.
  7. The fix: the condition moves into ON (1m). "Conditions on the right-hand table of a LEFT JOIN belong in ON." 
    SELECT m.name, COUNT(l.member_id) AS june_loans
FROM demo_members AS m
LEFT JOIN demo_member_loans AS l
       ON l.member_id = m.id AND l.loaned_on >= '2026-06-01'
GROUP BY m.id, m.name
ORDER BY m.name;

DROP DATABASE pitfall_demo;
    +-------+------------+
| name  | june_loans |
+-------+------------+
| Kevin |          0 |
| Rosa  |          1 |
| Tessa |          0 |
+-------+------------+
    The DROP at the end removes the scratch database. Leave the four error numbers on the board: 1064, 3780, 3814, 1824.
Studio: Milestone 1, Design (30 minutes)
  • Task (lesson, Milestone 1): on paper or in a text file, the tables with their columns, types and keys; the relationships (a sketch like the lesson's diagram is enough); and each of Rosa's eight requirements next to where the design enforces or answers it. No SQL yet.
  • Use the workbook's Milestone 1 page: the eight-row requirement table and the starter schema checklist (primary keys, named foreign keys with matching types and a deliberate ON DELETE, NOT NULL and UNIQUE, ENUM or lookup table with a reason, DECIMAL and DATE, two or more CHECKs, indexes for the open-loan lookups, no derived data, snake_case plural names).
  • Check-in (2 minutes each, from minute 10): the learner points at each requirement and says where it lives. Pass = all eight have a home and requirement 5's five rules are in a procedure, not in CHECKs. Ask one "why" question: "why an ENUM there?", "what happens to a member's loans if the member is deleted?". Tick the tracker.
  • Common design slips to coach: a courses text column in members ("how do you store the date for each course?"); available or times_borrowed columns ("what if a return is recorded and the flag is not reset?"); one name column for tools AND members in one table; ON DELETE CASCADE on loans ("Rosa said nobody may delete history"); no returned_on ("how do you know a loan is open?").
  • Fast finishers: compare with the reference design on the lesson page (open it now) and write one difference they can defend in their notes; then start Milestone 2.
Guided lab (15 minutes)

Files: labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/gift_cards_starter.sql, then labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/debug_gift_card_proofs.sql; reference labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/gift_cards_solution.sql. New practice on the bookstore (reload it first: SOURCE labs/_states/bookstore.sql): a gift-card feature with two tables, built like Milestone 2 asks: re-runnable, parents first, every constraint named.

  • Run it as shipped (2m): four constraints come back (two foreign keys on gift_card_uses, the amount CHECK and PRIMARY on gift_cards) and a TODO line for the balances.
  • TODOs 1 to 4 (8m): one named constraint each: uq_gift_cards_code, fk_gift_cards_customer, chk_gift_cards_dates (two columns, never CURDATE), the junction's composite PRIMARY KEY and chk_gcu_amount. Expected: nine constraints, three CHECK clauses. TODO 5 (the balance, derived with LEFT JOIN and COALESCE) is for fast finishers.
  • Then the proofs (5m): learners write the expected error number next to each test in debug_gift_card_proofs.sql, then run it. Expected: 3819, 3819, 1452, 1062, 1265, 1452 in that order, one row inside the transaction, zero after ROLLBACK. Ask: "which test would have passed silently before your TODOs?" (B, C and D.)
  • Debrief (1m): "This file is your Milestone 2 test block in miniature. Copy the shape: START TRANSACTION, one good parent row, one bad statement per rule, ROLLBACK, a count."
Studio: Milestone 2, Build the schema (40 minutes)
  • Mini-demo first (5m), the test pattern from the lesson: with the reference schema loaded on the projector (run the lesson's 01_schema.sql in your tool-library terminal), show the transaction and two of the four tests. The real outputs:
START TRANSACTION;

INSERT INTO categories (name) VALUES ('Hand Tools');
INSERT INTO members (first_name, last_name, email, joined_on)
VALUES ('Test', 'Member', 'test@example.org', '2026-01-01');
INSERT INTO tools (name, category_id, replacement_value, acquired_on)
VALUES ('Test hammer', 1, 15.00, '2026-01-01');
INSERT INTO loans (tool_id, member_id, loaned_on, due_on)
VALUES (1, 1, '2026-06-10', '2026-06-03');
ERROR 3819 (HY000): Check constraint 'chk_loans_due' is violated.
INSERT INTO tools (name, category_id, replacement_value, acquired_on)
VALUES ('Mystery tool', 99, 10.00, '2026-01-01');
ERROR 1452 (23000): Cannot add or update a child row: a foreign key constraint fails (`tool_library`.`tools`, CONSTRAINT `fk_tools_category` FOREIGN KEY (`category_id`) REFERENCES `categories` (`id`))
ROLLBACK;

SELECT COUNT(*) AS members_left FROM members;
+--------------+
| members_left |
+--------------+
|            0 |
+--------------+
  • Say: "a failed statement inside a transaction only undoes itself; ROLLBACK removes the good rows too. CREATE TABLE cannot be rolled back, which is why the tests come after the schema."
  • Task (lesson, Milestone 2): 01_schema.sql starting with DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS tool_library;, tables parents first, every constraint named, the open-loan indexes; then one statement each constraint must reject.
  • Check-in (from minute 25): the learner runs SOURCE 01_schema.sql twice in a row (no ERROR either time), then the test block (every test refused, members_left or their equivalent 0). Tick the tracker.
  • Coach: a FK with a different type (3780) or a parent created later (1824): the demo's numbers on the board; a test that is NOT refused (a missing constraint: "which rule did this test expect?"); constraints without names (the error then says loans_chk_1: ask them to name it so the error explains itself).
Studio: Milestone 3, Test data (25 minutes)
  • Task (lesson, Milestone 3): 02_data.sql that first runs SOURCE 01_schema.sql, then about 8 members, 5 categories, 2 courses, 12 tools and 15 or more loans over several months, with explicit ids and the planted edge cases: a suspended member, a member who never borrowed, a tool in repair, a tool never lent, a tie for most popular in a category, loans open, overdue and returned late.
  • Learners following the reference data get exactly these counts from the lesson's last query (real run):
+----------------+-----------+
| table_name     | row_count |
+----------------+-----------+
| members        |         8 |
| categories     |         5 |
| safety_courses |         2 |
| member_courses |         7 |
| tools          |        12 |
| loans          |        16 |
+----------------+-----------+
  • Check-in: the counts query, then "show me your suspended member, your never-borrowed member and your overdue loan". Learners with their own data must be able to point at each edge case by id.
  • Coach: 1452 halfway through (a child row before its parent's INSERT, or an id typo); 3819 from chk_loans_due (dates swapped: the CHECK just caught a real data error, say so); loans for the suspended member that start AFTER the suspension (fine, but say how the story goes) and two open loans for the same tool (the procedure will refuse that later, but INSERT does not: ask them to fix the data).
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • Rules that look at other rows written as CHECKs. "CHECK ((SELECT COUNT(*) ...) < 3)" fails (subqueries are not allowed in a CHECK). Coach with the board's last line: "one row or other rows?" and move the rule to the Milestone 5 procedure, written down now in the design notes.
  • Scripts that only run once. A learner fixes a column with ALTER TABLE at the prompt and the script no longer matches the database. Coach: "change the file, run the file": the rubric's Schema row and Milestone 8's rebuild depend on it.
  • Happy-path test data. Every member borrowed, nothing overdue. Coach with the edge-cases lab and Q10: "which of your queries would return nothing? Is that the right answer, or is the data hiding a bug?"
Checks for understanding

Ask these out loud during the check-ins:

  • Where does "at most three tools at once" live, and why not a CHECK? Answer: in checkout_tool, inside a transaction: it counts other rows, and a CHECK sees only the row being written.
  • Which table carries completed_on, and why there? Answer: member_courses: it is a fact about the pair member + course.
  • Why does a failed test INSERT not stop your test block? Answer: a failed statement only undoes itself; the transaction stays open until ROLLBACK.
  • Which of your tables would you create last, and drop first? Answer: loans (it points at tools and members), and member_courses.

Workbook self-check, items Q1 to Q12 (learners do them at home, after Milestone 3).

Answers: Q1 C) In checkout_tool, inside a transaction that locks the tool's row; Q2 B) completed_on, the date the member finished the course; Q3 One row only: Ana with 2 (Ben and Cy are gone); Q4 B) ERROR 3780 (HY000): Referencing column 'tool_id' and referenced column 'id' in foreign key constraint 'fk_loans_tool' are incompatible.; Q5 A) So the script can be run again from scratch and always gives the same result; Q6 C) The number of days a loan is overdue; Q7 0 (in a column named courses_left); Q8 B) categories, members, tools, loans; Q9 A) The loans foreign keys have no ON DELETE rule (refuse), and no account is granted DELETE; Q10 D) The test data has no overdue loan and every member has borrowed something; Q11 The library adds and lists categories (they are data); the conditions are a fixed list the code relies on; Q12 A) ERROR 1064 (42000): You have an error in your SQL syntax ... near 'condition VARCHAR(10). Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: learners who struggle with the design start from the lesson's reference design table (open the hidden block) and write the "why" for each row in their own words: that is honest Design work at the "meets" level. In Milestone 2, pair them for the test block. A learner far behind can type the reference 01_schema.sql and then write their OWN tests (the tests are where the understanding shows).

Stretch: the lesson's Exercise 4 idea early (a generated column that makes "one open loan per tool" a UNIQUE constraint); the FAQ's alternative clinic brief as a second design; a lookup table for tool conditions instead of the ENUM, with the trade-off written down.

Closing

Exit ticket (3 questions, no notes, 4 minutes)

On the slide. Learners answer in the workbook's Exit Ticket box or on a sticky note. Sort into "got it" / "not yet" before Session 14: question 2's pile is the list for the Session 14 warm-up.

  1. Name one of Rosa's rules that must live in a procedure, and say why it cannot be a CHECK. Answer: any of: one open loan per tool, at most three per member, course before the chainsaw, not while in repair, not while suspended: each looks at other rows (or another table), and a CHECK sees only the row being written.
  2. Your 01_schema.sql ran once. What must be true for it to run a second time without an error? Answer: it starts with DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS tool_library; (or drops its tables, children first) and creates everything again.
  3. Write one test statement that proves a CHECK on your loans table works. What should MySQL answer? Answer: for example an INSERT with due_on before loaned_on; MySQL must answer ERROR 3819 ... Check constraint 'chk_loans_due' is violated.
Homework (about 3 to 5 hours)
  • Finish Milestones 1 to 3 if the room did not: the design with every requirement mapped, 01_schema.sql running twice with its tests, 02_data.sql with the edge cases and the counts query.
  • Milestone 4, the Saturday questions (03_queries.sql): six commented queries, all with SET @today = '2026-06-15';, at least two checked by hand against the data. Learners on the reference data can compare with the lesson's hidden reference; everyone writes down the two hand checks.
  • Lab: labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/edge_cases_starter.sql: four report queries that only edge cases can test (an inner join that should be a LEFT JOIN, a WHERE that should be in ON, ROW_NUMBER that should be RANK). It is Milestone 4's patterns on the bookstore.
  • Optional: labs/module-13/l8_2-final-project/debug_build_order.sql (four build mistakes in a bike-share script); the self-check Q1 to Q12 in the workbook.
  • Journal: the lesson's prompt can wait for Session 14; tonight write which requirement was hardest to place and where you put it.
  • Read ahead for Session 14: Milestones 5 to 7 on the lesson page (try them first, the reference stays hidden) and Lesson 7.2's EXIT HANDLER pattern.
Bring list for Session 14
  • The tool-library folder with 01_schema.sql, 02_data.sql and 03_queries.sql that rebuild the database from scratch (Session 14 starts by running them).
  • The design notes and the printed rubric.
  • A laptop with MySQL 9.7 and the bookstore loaded (the Session 14 labs use it), and the root password.

If You Run Long or Short

Running long: what to protect and what to cut first
  • Protect: a passed design check-in for every learner; 01_schema.sql running twice with its tests; the demo's Mistake 5.
  • Cut first (saves 15m): the guided lab. Hand out the gift-cards files as homework; the Milestone 2 mini-demo shows the test pattern.
  • Cut second (saves 6m): demo Mistakes 2 and 4. Point at the lesson page and the trap table.
  • Cut third (saves 25m): the Milestone 3 studio: it becomes homework (it is mostly typing). Make sure everyone has seen the edge-case list on the slide.
Running short: what to add
  • Start Milestone 4 together: query 1 (on the shelf now) with NOT EXISTS, checked by hand against the data (7 of 12 tools for the reference data).
  • Run debug_build_order.sql for everyone; each fix is one volunteer.
  • Show the reference design and let pairs argue for one difference in their own design (two minutes each).
Instructor Kit · Facilitator Guide

Session 14: Final Project Studio II, Showcase and Next Steps

How to run the last session of MySQL Foundations. Studio II takes the final project through business logic, least-privilege accounts and a backup proven by a restore; every learner presents in the showcase; Lesson 8.3 turns the course into habits (SQL injection and placeholders, an information_schema health check pointed at their own project) and the course closes with submission, evaluation and next steps.

2 lessons 3 hrs session Lessons 8.2–8.3 · Milestones 5–7 Session 14 of 14 · Part 2

How to Use This Guide

  • Session 14 closes the course. Before the break: Studio II (Milestones 5 to 7 of the final project, Lesson 8.2), with a short frame and demo. After the break: the showcase (every learner presents for three minutes), then Lesson 8.3 (best practices, the health check, SQL injection, what to learn next) and the course close.
  • Every SQL block below was run for real on MySQL 9.7. The 8.2 outputs come from the lesson's own reference solution run as one session (they match the page); the account logins and the dump and restore were measured by logging in as the accounts and running mysqldump. The 8.3 outputs come from the lesson page's own blocks on a fresh bookstore.
  • Keep lesson_final_project.html, lesson_21.html and final-assessment.html open. The final-assessment page holds the rubric, scoring guidance, the reference numbers for every milestone and the submission rules.
  • Prepare the projector copy before class: in a terminal started inside a folder holding the lesson's reference scripts, build tool_library (Milestones 2, 3 and 5's procedures, not the calls), so the demo's calls answer exactly as above. Do not show the scripts themselves.
  • Teal blocks are hands-on, amber blocks are watch-outs. Lesson codes match the course site.
The #1 risk of Session 14: no time left for the showcase and the close

Milestone 5 expands to fill any time you give it. The showcase and Lesson 8.3 are what turn a project into a finished course, so they are fixed. The defense: at 1:05 everyone moves to Milestone 6 whatever the state of their procedure (a procedure that enforces three rules is "meets" on the rubric; the rest goes home). At 1:30 the break starts on time. A learner whose procedure is broken presents a design decision and a query instead.

Before the Session

  • ☐ Projector copy of the reference: tool_library built from the lesson's scripts (see How to Use), and the bookstore loaded fresh in a second terminal for the 8.3 demo and the labs.
  • ☐ Files to hand out: labs/module-14/ without the *_solution.sql files. Run python3 labs/check_labs.py module-14 once (10 files, every line PASS).
  • ☐ Showcase order: a list on the board or a shared document; a timer visible to the room (3 minutes, then 30 seconds of questions). Groups larger than ten: run two showcase rooms or a gallery walk (see the showcase block).
  • ☐ Print or share: the showcase cards ("one strength, one question", on sticky notes), the workbook (its showcase page and course-close page), the submission instructions from final-assessment.html, and the course evaluation link.
  • ☐ Board, before learners arrive: the five-box procedure shape; "Milestone 6 at 1:05 · break 1:30 · showcase 1:40"; the three showcase prompts (a design decision · a Saturday answer checked by hand · a rule refused live).
  • ☐ Certificates or completion records, if your program issues them: the completion rule is in final-assessment.html (the project is submitted after this session, so completion is confirmed after grading).
Known traps in this session
SymptomCauseFix
CREATE PROCEDURE ends at the first ;No DELIMITER // before it (or a missing DELIMITER ; after)Lesson 7.1's DELIMITER lines around every procedure in 04_logic.sql.
A failed check-out leaves the session in an open transaction (later statements behave oddly)No EXIT HANDLER, or a handler without ROLLBACKDECLARE EXIT HANDLER FOR SQLEXCEPTION BEGIN ROLLBACK; RESIGNAL; END; as the last DECLARE.
ERROR 1337 (42000): Variable or condition declaration after cursor or handler declarationThe handler declared before a variable (measured on 9.7)Variables first, then the handler; every DECLARE before the first statement.
A call for a member that does not exist fails with 1452 instead of a clear messageNo IS NULL test: a SELECT ... INTO that finds no row leaves the variable NULL with only a warning, and NULL <> 'active' is not true, so the INSERT runs and the foreign key refuses itTest IS NULL first after each SELECT ... INTO, as the reference does ('No such member').
tl_desk_app gets 1142 on everything, even SELECTThe role was granted but not activatedSET DEFAULT ROLE 'tl_desk' TO 'tl_desk_app'@'localhost'; (Lesson 7.4's silent mistake); log in again.
mysqldump: command not found at the mysql> prompt, or ERROR 1064mysqldump typed inside the clientIt is a terminal command (Lesson 8.1).
Restore fails with a DEFINER or privilege errorRestoring as a non-root accountRestore as root (the lesson says so): the view and procedures carry DEFINER=root@localhost.
Learners with their own data get other counts in Milestone 7ExpectedThe check is that the two counts match each other, not that they are 18.
