In the room. Lesson 8.2's Milestones 5, 6 and 7 (the lesson estimates 40 + 20 + 20 minutes) get 65 minutes plus a 15-minute frame; the showcase takes 35. Lesson 8.3 (80 minutes for a solo learner) gets 35 minutes: the frame, the injection and health-check demo, and Steps 1 to 4 of its project on the learner's own tool_library. The rest of 8.3 goes home.

Goes home (about 2 to 3 hours, plus the project). Finish the final project: Milestones 5 to 7 if unfinished, Milestone 8 (rebuild from the files on a clean server, the self-review list) and README.md; submit within one week. Lesson 8.2's Exercises 1 to 3 (they run on tool_library as Milestone 5 leaves it), both Quick Quizzes, both journal entries (the 8.2 prompt about a changed design decision; the 8.3 prompt looking back at Lesson 1.1). Lesson 8.3's project Step 5 (learning_plan.md) and Exercises 1 and 2.

Protect, in this order: the showcase for every learner; Milestone 6 (accounts that are not root, no DELETE); the 8.3 injection demo; the course close. Cut first: the 8.3 health-check demo steps (the project start runs the same checks), then the Milestone 5 frame's success calls, then Milestone 7 in the room (the commands go home with the reference numbers from the slide).

Opening Segment

0:00

Warm-up: the Rebuild Check

10 min
Timing
5mRebuild check: 02_data.sql, then 03_queries.sql, from the tool-library folder
5mThree questions on Milestone 4 (answers in the Answer Key)
Warm-up questions
W1
From a client started in your tool-library folder: SOURCE 02_data.sql, then SOURCE 03_queries.sql. Which line fails, if any? (Run your scripts)
W2
Saturday question 3 uses RANK(). What would ROW_NUMBER() lose? (Milestone 4)
W3
Why does every Saturday query read @today instead of CURDATE()? (Milestone 4)
Sequence and talking points
  • Rebuild check (5m). Say: "Start your client inside tool-library and rebuild everything from your files. If anything needs a manual fix, that is your first job today." Walk the room once; learners whose scripts fail go on the tracker for the first visit of the studio.
  • Milestone 4 (5m). W2 and W3 by cold call; then ask two learners to read a hand check aloud ("7 of 12 tools on the shelf: 2, 4, 7 and 10 are out and 12 is in repair"). Learners on the reference data compare with the hidden reference; learners with their own data compare with their hand checks.
  • Learners who did not do Milestone 4: they do Milestone 5 today anyway (it does not depend on the queries) and Milestone 4 at home. The showcase can show a query from Milestone 2's tests instead.

Lesson Plans

8.2

Final Project: Design and Build a Database

115 min (of 240)
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Turn a written brief into entities, relationships and business rules, and draw them as a diagram
  • Build a normalized schema in a re-runnable script, with keys, foreign keys and CHECK constraints that you have tested
  • Answer business questions with joins, GROUP BY, CTEs and window functions, using a fixed report date
  • Enforce rules that span several rows in a stored procedure with a transaction
  • Secure the database with roles and least-privilege accounts, then back it up and prove the backup restores

Today: the last two objectives in the room (rules in a procedure with a transaction; roles, accounts, a backup that is proven to restore), and every objective in the showcase.

Timing in the room
15mFrame and live demo: the shape of checkout_tool; one failing call per rule
40mStudio: Milestone 5, 04_logic.sql
25mStudio: Milestones 6 and 7, 05_security.sql, a login as the desk account, the backup and the restore test
35mShowcase (after the break)
Mental model to say

"Last session you sorted the rules: one row → a constraint, other rows → a procedure. Today you write that procedure. It is the only door into the loans table: the desk's account may not INSERT into loans at all, only CALL checkout_tool, and the procedure runs with its definer's rights, so every loan passes through every rule."

"A procedure that checks first and inserts second has a gap: two desk volunteers can both see the drill as free. The transaction and the row lock close the gap; the handler makes sure a refusal undoes everything and tells the caller why."

Live demo (15 minutes; show the answers, not the code)
  1. The shape, on the board (no code yet) (4m). Draw five boxes in a column and say each one: "START TRANSACTION · read and LOCK the rows the rules need (SELECT ... FOR UPDATE) · check each rule, SIGNAL a clear message when one fails · INSERT · COMMIT. Around it all, one EXIT HANDLER: ROLLBACK, then RESIGNAL." Point at the five rules from requirement 5 and ask which row each one reads (member: status; tool: condition and course; loans: open loan for this tool, open loans for this member; member_courses). Do not show the reference procedure: Milestone 5 is the learners' own work. Lesson 7.2's handler and Lesson 7.1's SIGNAL are the two pieces they already have.
  2. What "proven" looks like: one failing call per rule (6m). "This is the reference procedure answering. Yours must give each rule its own message." Run the calls on your projector copy (built from the lesson's scripts before class). 
    CALL checkout_tool(8, 1, '2026-06-15');   -- Victor is suspended
CALL checkout_tool(1, 12, '2026-06-15');  -- tile cutter in repair
CALL checkout_tool(6, 7, '2026-06-15');   -- ladder is out with Amara
CALL checkout_tool(7, 6, '2026-06-15');   -- Tessa has no chainsaw course
    ERROR 1644 (45000): Member is suspended
ERROR 1644 (45000): Tool is out for repair
ERROR 1644 (45000): Tool is already on loan
ERROR 1644 (45000): Safety course required for this tool
    Four rules, four messages, error 1644 each (SQLSTATE 45000 is "user-defined").
  3. The limit, and a success (3m). "Kevin already has the socket set. Two more succeed, the fourth must not." 
    CALL checkout_tool(2, 1, '2026-06-15');
CALL checkout_tool(2, 3, '2026-06-15');
CALL checkout_tool(2, 8, '2026-06-15');   -- a fourth tool for Kevin
    +---------+------------+
| loan_id | due_on     |
+---------+------------+
|      17 | 2026-06-22 |
+---------+------------+
+---------+------------+
| loan_id | due_on     |
+---------+------------+
|      18 | 2026-06-22 |
+---------+------------+
ERROR 1644 (45000): Loan limit reached (3 open loans)
    The successful call prints the new loan id and due date: the desk's web form can show them.
  4. Returns and the desk view (2m). "return_tool closes the open loan; the view is the desk's screen." 
    CALL return_tool(1, '2026-06-16');
CALL return_tool(3, '2026-06-16');
CALL return_tool(7, '2026-06-16');

SELECT * FROM v_open_loans ORDER BY due_on, loan_id;

CALL return_tool(7, '2026-06-16');        -- the ladder is already back
    +---------+---------+----------------+--------------+------------+------------+
| loan_id | tool_id | tool           | member       | loaned_on  | due_on     |
+---------+---------+----------------+--------------+------------+------------+
|      14 |       2 | Cordless drill | Rosa Delgado | 2026-06-06 | 2026-06-13 |
|      15 |       4 | Socket set     | Kevin Brooks | 2026-06-10 | 2026-06-17 |
|      16 |      10 | Carpet cleaner | Hannah Weiss | 2026-06-12 | 2026-06-19 |
+---------+---------+----------------+--------------+------------+------------+
ERROR 1644 (45000): Tool is not on loan
    18 loans now: 16 from the data plus Kevin's two. Milestone 7's counts use this number.
Studio: Milestone 5, Business logic (40 minutes)
  • Task (lesson, Milestone 5): 04_logic.sql with the view v_open_loans, checkout_tool(member, tool, date) enforcing all five rules of requirement 5 inside a transaction with a clear message for each, and return_tool(tool, date); NULL as the date means today. Then a call that must fail for every rule and one that must succeed.
  • Stuck on the shape? Send them to labs/module-14/l8_2-final-project/add_item_rules_starter.sql first (15 minutes): the same shape on the bookstore (lock the order, check, SIGNAL, insert, commit, the handler), with labs/module-14/l8_2-final-project/debug_add_item_rules.sql as its failing calls. Then back to the project.
  • Check-in (from minute 25): the learner runs their failing calls: each rule refused with its own message (five messages), one success, and SELECT * FROM v_open_loans afterwards. Ask: "what happens to the transaction when the course check fails?" (the handler rolls it back).
  • Coach: a rule checked AFTER the INSERT (the loan exists before the refusal: move the checks up); a COUNT that includes returned loans (returned_on IS NULL missing); loaned_on + 7 instead of + INTERVAL 7 DAY (it turns the date into a number: measured, DATE('2026-06-28') + 7 is 20260635, not a date); checking v_condition = 'repair' when no tool was found (test IS NULL first).
  • At 1:05 everyone moves on, finished or not (see the #1 risk).
Studio: Milestones 6 and 7, Security and backup (25 minutes)
  • Milestone 6 (12m): 05_security.sql with the roles tl_desk and tl_reports and the two accounts, each with SET DEFAULT ROLE. Then log in as the desk account in a second terminal and try what it must not do. The reference, measured:
SHOW GRANTS FOR 'tl_desk_app'@'localhost' USING 'tl_desk';
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Grants for tl_desk_app@localhost                                                       |
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| GRANT USAGE ON *.* TO `tl_desk_app`@`localhost`                                        |
| GRANT SELECT ON `tool_library`.* TO `tl_desk_app`@`localhost`                          |
| GRANT INSERT, UPDATE ON `tool_library`.`members` TO `tl_desk_app`@`localhost`          |
| GRANT EXECUTE ON PROCEDURE `tool_library`.`checkout_tool` TO `tl_desk_app`@`localhost` |
| GRANT EXECUTE ON PROCEDURE `tool_library`.`return_tool` TO `tl_desk_app`@`localhost`   |
| GRANT `tl_desk`@`%` TO `tl_desk_app`@`localhost`                                       |
+----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
-- logged in as tl_desk_app:
CALL checkout_tool(4, 1, '2026-06-17');
DELETE FROM loans WHERE id = 1;
INSERT INTO loans (tool_id, member_id, loaned_on, due_on) VALUES (3, 4, '2026-06-17', '2026-06-24');
+---------+------------+
| loan_id | due_on     |
+---------+------------+
|      19 | 2026-06-24 |
+---------+------------+
ERROR 1142 (42000): DELETE command denied to user 'tl_desk_app'@'localhost' for table 'loans'
ERROR 1142 (42000): INSERT command denied to user 'tl_desk_app'@'localhost' for table 'loans'
  • The CALL works (definer's rights); the direct DELETE and INSERT are refused. The treasurer's account reads (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM loans answers) and is refused an UPDATE with the same 1142.
  • Milestone 7 (13m): in the terminal, the lesson's mysqldump command, CREATE DATABASE tool_library_test, the restore, then the comparison in the client. Measured on the reference after Milestone 5 (338-line dump, no errors):
+----------------+------------+
| loans_original | loans_copy |
+----------------+------------+
|             18 |         18 |
+----------------+------------+
  • Both counts 18; checkout_tool and return_tool are in the copy (defined by root@localhost); the copy's v_open_loans lists 3 open loans. Then DROP DATABASE tool_library_test; and one line in the README with the date and the counts.
  • Check-in: the learner shows SHOW GRANTS for the desk account (no DELETE, no INSERT on loans) and the two matching counts.
Showcase (35 minutes, after the break)

Three minutes per learner (pairs: four minutes per pair), then 30 seconds for one question. Every presenter shows three things, from their own laptop on the projector or as screenshots:

  1. One design decision and why (an ENUM or a lookup table, a foreign key's ON DELETE rule, where a rule lives).
  2. One Saturday answer, checked by hand ("7 of 12 on the shelf: here is why").
  3. One rule refused live (a failing CALL, or a constraint test).

Listeners write one strength and one question on a sticky note per presenter and hand them over; you fill the showcase sheet below (one strength per learner goes into the written feedback). The showcase is practice: it is not scored. Rubric scores come from the submitted files.

  • More than ten learners: split into two rooms with a co-facilitator, or run a gallery walk (laptops open at the three things, half the room presents while the other half walks, then swap: 2 × 15 minutes).
  • A learner whose procedure is broken shows a design decision, a query and a constraint test instead. Nobody skips the showcase.
  • Close the showcase with one minute from you: the three strengths you saw most often, and the one fix you saw most often (it goes in everybody's README as a next step).
Showcase sheet
LearnerDesign decision (and why)Saturday answer, checked by handRule refused liveOne strength to write in the feedback
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • Checks without a lock. The procedure checks and inserts correctly, but reads the tool without FOR UPDATE. Coach with two terminals: "open a transaction in each and lend the same drill" (Lesson 7.2's two-session demo). Q5 in the self-check.
  • root everywhere. The desk account was skipped, or created with ALL PRIVILEGES. Coach with Rosa's requirement 7 and the rubric's Security row ("desk account can only change data through procedures").
  • A backup that was never restored. "The dump file exists" is the "meets" level; full points need the restore and the matching counts in the README. Coach: "a backup is only a backup once it has been restored."
Checks for understanding

Ask during the studio check-ins:

  • Why can the desk account create a loan it may not INSERT? Answer: the procedure runs with its definer's rights (SQL SECURITY DEFINER, the default).
  • What does RESIGNAL add to ROLLBACK? Answer: the caller still receives the original error and message.
  • Your dump restored. What else must you keep to rebuild Rosa's system? Answer: 05_security.sql (the accounts are not in the dump) and the numbered scripts.

Workbook self-check, items Q1 to Q6.

Answers: Q1 C) ERROR 1644 (45000): Safety course required for this tool; Q2 A) So a failed check-out never leaves a half-finished transaction, and the caller still receives the real error message; Q3 ERROR 1142 (42000): DELETE command denied to user 'tl_desk_app'@'localhost' for table 'loans'; Q4 D) The accounts tl_desk_app and tl_treasurer and their roles; Q5 B) So a second check-out of the same tool waits until the first commits, then sees the new loan; Q6 Four rows; loan 13 (the extension ladder, 12 days) first. Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: the add_item_rules lab first; then a procedure with three rules ("meets" on Business logic) is a fine result for today. In Milestone 6, the lesson's reference 05_security.sql may be typed and then TESTED by the learner: the login test is where the understanding shows.

Stretch: the lesson's Exercise 4 (a generated column that makes one open loan per tool a UNIQUE constraint), the Bonus Challenge (reservations), or a scheduled backup (Lesson 8.1 Step 6) for tool_library.

8.3

Next Steps & Best Practices

35 min
Objectives (from the lesson page)
  • Summarize what each module of the course taught and where to look things up again
  • Apply a short list of design, query, security and operations practices to any MySQL project
  • Audit a database with information_schema queries for missing keys, risky types and old character sets
  • Explain SQL injection and write parameterized queries, in SQL and in an application language
  • Choose a next learning step that fits your goal
Timing in the room
5mFrame: the course in one page; habits, not rules
15mLive demo: SQL injection and the placeholder; information_schema checks; safe-update mode
15mStart the lesson project: health_check.sql on tool_library (Steps 1 to 4)
Mental model to say

Show the lesson's module table ("The Course in One Page") and say: "Every practice in this lesson prevents a problem you met in this course: a missing primary key, FLOAT money, an UPDATE without WHERE, a password in code. Habits are cheap insurance."

Then the one idea that matters most when they leave: "Real users never see a mysql> prompt. Applications send SQL through a driver, and the user's input travels as a PARAMETER, separate from the SQL text. Glue input into SQL and someone else writes your WHERE clause."

Live demo (you type, they follow; 15 minutes)

The lesson's own blocks on a freshly loaded bookstore; outputs are real and match the page.

  1. Building SQL from input: the leak (5m). "A web form looks up a customer by the email someone typed. Here is what gluing the input into the SQL does when the input is crafted." Read the query_sent line aloud. 
    SET @input = 'nobody@example.com'' OR ''1''=''1';

SET @sql = CONCAT('SELECT first_name, email FROM customers WHERE email = ''', @input, '''');
SELECT @sql AS query_sent;

PREPARE lookup FROM @sql;
EXECUTE lookup;
DEALLOCATE PREPARE lookup;
    +---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| query_sent                                                                            |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| SELECT first_name, email FROM customers WHERE email = 'nobody@example.com' OR '1'='1' |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
Statement prepared
+------------+-------------------+
| first_name | email             |
+------------+-------------------+
| Alice      | alice@example.com |
| Bob        | bob@example.com   |
| Carol      | carol@example.com |
| Dave       | dave@example.com  |
| Eve        | eve@example.com   |
| Frank      | frank@example.com |
| Grace      | grace@example.com |
| Hank       | hank@example.com  |
+------------+-------------------+
    Every customer's email leaks, with no error. Variations delete tables or skip logins.
  2. The fix: a placeholder (3m). "The value travels separately and can only ever be a value. Every driver does this." 
    PREPARE lookup FROM 'SELECT first_name, email FROM customers WHERE email = ?';
EXECUTE lookup USING @input;
DEALLOCATE PREPARE lookup;
    Statement prepared
Empty set
    Show one driver example on the lesson page (the language your group uses next): ?, %s, :name, @name are all the same idea.
  3. information_schema checks a design (5m). "Every server describes itself in tables. Plant an old-style table, then let a query find it." 
    CREATE TABLE hc_demo_payments (
    paid_on DATE,
    amount  FLOAT,
    note    VARCHAR(100) CHARACTER SET latin1
);

SELECT t.TABLE_NAME AS table_without_pk
FROM information_schema.TABLES AS t
LEFT JOIN information_schema.TABLE_CONSTRAINTS AS c
       ON c.TABLE_SCHEMA = t.TABLE_SCHEMA
      AND c.TABLE_NAME = t.TABLE_NAME
      AND c.CONSTRAINT_TYPE = 'PRIMARY KEY'
WHERE t.TABLE_SCHEMA = 'bookstore'
  AND t.TABLE_TYPE = 'BASE TABLE'
  AND c.CONSTRAINT_NAME IS NULL
ORDER BY t.TABLE_NAME;
    +------------------+
| table_without_pk |
+------------------+
| hc_demo_payments |
+------------------+
  4. Risky types (2m). "FLOAT for money and latin1 text." 
    SELECT TABLE_NAME, COLUMN_NAME, COLUMN_TYPE, CHARACTER_SET_NAME
FROM information_schema.COLUMNS
WHERE TABLE_SCHEMA = 'bookstore'
  AND (DATA_TYPE IN ('float', 'double')
       OR CHARACTER_SET_NAME NOT IN ('utf8mb4', 'binary'))
ORDER BY TABLE_NAME, ORDINAL_POSITION;
    +------------------+-------------+--------------+--------------------+
| TABLE_NAME       | COLUMN_NAME | COLUMN_TYPE  | CHARACTER_SET_NAME |
+------------------+-------------+--------------+--------------------+
| hc_demo_payments | amount      | float        | NULL               |
| hc_demo_payments | note        | varchar(100) | latin1             |
+------------------+-------------+--------------+--------------------+
    Then DROP TABLE hc_demo_payments;. FLOAT for money: the lesson's demo sums 0.10 + 0.20 + 19.99 to 20.289999775588512.
  5. Safe-update mode (2m). "A forgotten WHERE, caught." 
    SET SQL_SAFE_UPDATES = 1;

UPDATE books SET price = price * 1.10;
    ERROR 1175 (HY000): You are using safe update mode and you tried to update a table without a WHERE that uses a KEY column.
    Then SET SQL_SAFE_UPDATES = 0;.
Start the lesson project: Health Check (15 minutes)
  • Steps 1 and 2: health_check.sql with SET @db = 'bookstore'; and the three checks; on the bookstore, checks 1 and 2 print Empty set (the result you want) and the table count is 8.
  • Step 3: the unindexed-foreign-key check (Empty set on the bookstore).
  • Step 4, the point of today: SET @db = 'tool_library'; and run it on their own project. On the reference tool_library (measured): 6 tables; no table without a primary key; no FLOAT or non-utf8mb4 column; every foreign-key column leads an index; and the delete rules:
+----------------+-------------------+-----------------------+-------------+
| TABLE_NAME     | CONSTRAINT_NAME   | REFERENCED_TABLE_NAME | DELETE_RULE |
+----------------+-------------------+-----------------------+-------------+
| loans          | fk_loans_member   | members               | NO ACTION   |
| loans          | fk_loans_tool     | tools                 | NO ACTION   |
| member_courses | fk_mc_course      | safety_courses        | NO ACTION   |
| member_courses | fk_mc_member      | members               | CASCADE     |
| tools          | fk_tools_category | categories            | NO ACTION   |
| tools          | fk_tools_course   | safety_courses        | NO ACTION   |
+----------------+-------------------+-----------------------+-------------+
  • Ask: "why NO ACTION here and RESTRICT on the bookstore?" Both mean "refuse" in InnoDB; the bookstore's script wrote ON DELETE RESTRICT, the project wrote no rule at all, and information_schema reports the default as NO ACTION. Only fk_mc_member cascades: course records mean nothing without their member.
  • Goes home: Step 5 (learning_plan.md: a path, a first topic, a project, dates) and the comment block at the top of the file.
  • Fast finishers: labs/module-14/l8_3-next-steps/health_check_starter.sql (two checks the lesson does not have: _id columns that no foreign key protects, and the CASCADE/SET NULL list), labs/module-14/l8_3-next-steps/safe_search_starter.sql (placeholders on the bookstore) and labs/module-14/l8_3-next-steps/debug_unsafe_habits.sql (safe-update mode).
Three mistakes to expect, and how to coach each
  • "Escaping quotes is enough." Coach: placeholders cost nothing and cannot be gotten wrong; escaping by hand can. The lesson says so in its Common Mistake 1.
  • Health check reports nothing, so the database must be fine. Coach: run it on the planted table (the lab plants one) to see that each check CAN find something. An empty result only means something when the check can fail.
  • Learning plans with no dates. Coach with the lesson's Step 5: a path, one first topic, one small project and a date next to each.
Checks for understanding
  • What makes a parameterized query safe? Answer: the value is sent separately from the SQL text, so it can never become part of the statement.
  • Which information_schema table tells you what deleting a parent does? Answer: REFERENTIAL_CONSTRAINTS (DELETE_RULE).
  • Which MySQL version do you install for production next year? Answer: the current LTS (9.7 or 8.4), asked for by name.

Workbook self-check, items Q7 to Q12.

Answers: Q7 D) All eight customers; Q8 A) They are deleted too (fk_items_order is CASCADE); Q9 It is refused: ERROR 1175 (safe update mode: no KEY column in the WHERE); Q10 C) It is the "find what is missing" pattern: keep every table, then keep only those that found no PRIMARY KEY row; Q11 B) In an environment variable or a secrets manager, never in the code or the repository; Q12 EXECUTE stmt USING @author, @max_price;: user variables, in the same order as the ? placeholders. Full set with explanations in the Answer Key.

Differentiation

Support: the health-check starter has check 1 done; Steps 1 to 4 on the bookstore only are enough for today. Stretch: the lesson's Exercise 3 (a one-line summary per table with correlated subqueries) and its Bonus Challenge (a trigger, a FULLTEXT index or a scheduled event added to the final project, with one thing the manual taught them).

Closing

Course close (10 minutes)
  1. Final submission (3m). On the slide: the zipped tool-library folder (the five scripts, the backup file of the test data, README.md with the restore date and counts, the self-scored rubric), within one week, by the channel your program uses. Placeholder passwords only. The full rules: final-assessment.html.
  2. Exit ticket (3m). Three questions below.
  3. Course evaluation (2m). The link or the paper form; ask for one sentence on what to keep and one on what to change.
  4. What comes next (2m). Show "Choose a path by goal" from Lesson 8.3 once more, and the course's reference pages (cheat sheet, glossary, common errors, troubleshooting). Thank them; name one thing the group did well.
Exit ticket (3 questions, no notes, 3 minutes)
  1. Why can the desk's web form create a loan through checkout_tool although its account may not INSERT into loans? Answer: the procedure runs with its definer's rights (SQL SECURITY DEFINER is the default), so it may insert; the caller only needs EXECUTE, and every loan passes the rules.
  2. Rewrite CONCAT('... WHERE email = ''', @input, '''') safely. Answer: PREPARE s FROM 'SELECT ... WHERE email = ?'; EXECUTE s USING @input; (a driver: a placeholder plus the value in a list).
  3. Name your next step from Lesson 8.3's table and the date you start. Answer: any path with a date (the learning plan's first line).
Homework (about 2 to 3 hours, plus finishing the project)
  • Finish the final project and submit it within one week: Milestones 5 to 7 if unfinished; Milestone 8 (rebuild from the files in order on a clean server or after DROP DATABASE tool_library;, the self-review list); README.md (one page: design, rebuild, backups with the restore date and counts, next steps); score it with the rubric and write the score and one improvement in the README (the lesson's "Before the Next Lesson").
  • Lesson 8.2: Exercises 1 to 3 (late returns, member activity, value at risk; they run on tool_library as Milestone 5 leaves it, 18 loans); Exercise 4 is a stretch. The Quick Quiz. The journal prompt (a design decision you changed your mind about).
  • Lesson 8.3: project Step 5, learning_plan.md with dates; Exercises 1 and 2 (Exercise 3 is a stretch); the Quick Quiz; the journal prompt (look back at your Lesson 1.1 entry).
  • Optional labs: labs/module-14/l8_3-next-steps/safe_search_starter.sql, labs/module-14/l8_3-next-steps/health_check_starter.sql, labs/module-14/l8_2-final-project/least_privilege_starter.sql.
  • After the course: put the first date of the learning plan in a calendar; push the project and health_check.sql to a repository (placeholder passwords only, never a backup of real data, never .mylogin.cnf).

If You Run Long or Short

Running long: what to protect and what to cut first
  • Protect: the showcase for every learner; Milestone 6; the injection demo; the course close.
  • Cut first (saves 7m): the 8.3 demo's information_schema and safe-update steps: the project start runs the same checks.
  • Cut second (saves 3m): the 8.2 demo's success and return steps.
  • Cut third (saves 13m): Milestone 7 in the room. Show the slide with the commands and the measured counts; it goes home.
Running short: what to add
  • Two-terminal demo of the lock: open a transaction in each terminal and call checkout_tool for the same tool; the second waits, then reports "Tool is already on loan".
  • The 8.2 Exercise 3 (value at risk with ROLLUP) together, on the reference data: Cleaning 200.00, Hand Tools 60.00, Power Tools 120.00, all categories 380.00.
  • safe_search_starter.sql for everyone, then one learner explains the placeholder in their next language.
